> Verified against commit 0dd8e76 (2026-06-01) on 2026-10-02.

# Known issues: Creatures, templates and summoning (CRE-)

## Bugs and rules deviations

- **CRE-007** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Every swarm deals a flat 6 damage.** `AIService.RollSwarmDamage` returns `SwarmDamage` whenever it is above 0 and all six swarms set it to 6, so bat, rat and spider swarms always deal their 1d6 maximum and centipede, hellwasp and locust swarms (meant as 2d6/3d6) deal 6. RAW swarm damage is rolled (1d6 to 3d6), and `SwarmTraits.SwarmDamageCount` is never read. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs:1956-1960; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs:98-99 (bat), NPCDatabase_R.cs:46-47, NPCDatabase_S.cs:151-152, NPCDatabase_C.cs:376, NPCDatabase_H.cs:413, NPCDatabase_L.cs:539. *Fix:* set `SwarmDamage = 0` with the right `SwarmDamageDice`, and drop or wire up SwarmDamageCount.
- **CRE-008** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Celestial and fiendish templates change ability scores and gate SR by HD.** `OutsiderTemplateBase.ApplyStatAdjustments` raises STR, CON, CHA and WIS (celestial) or DEX (fiendish) but floors INT at 3 only for animals and magical beasts, and `ApplyMitigation` grants SR only at 8+ HD. RAW (MM 3.5): abilities unchanged except Int at least 3, SR HD+5 (max 25) at any HD; DR and energy resistance are correct. Flooring Int is not enough for a mindless base such as a vermin: MM p.31 and p.108 also make an animal or vermin with the template a magical beast (augmented), and a creature with an Int score is not mindless. The template copies `IsMindless` and the `ImmunityPresets.MindlessImmunities` preset unchanged and sets no `CreatureType`, so at spawn `GameManager.InitializeNPCFromDefinition` calls `CharacterStats.ApplyMindlessTrait(def.IsMindless)`, which sets INT to no score and immunity to mind-affecting effects, and `CharacterStats.IsImmuneToMindAffecting` grants the same immunity to the `vermin` type. A celestial or fiendish vermin summon (the giant fire beetle, monstrous centipedes and spiders, bombardier beetle, praying mantis and wasp, and since CRE-003 (2026-10-14) the Summon Monster II celestial giant bee, which until then was a dire bat clone with Int 2) is therefore immune to Sleep, Command, charms and the other mind-affecting spells, for PCs' and NPCs' summons alike, where RAW gives it Int 3 and no such immunity. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/CreatureTemplateFramework.cs:195-253 (`ApplyStatAdjustments`, `ApplyMitigation`); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs:933 (`stats.ApplyMindlessTrait(def.IsMindless)`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs (`ApplyMindlessTrait`, `IsImmuneToMindAffecting`). *Fix:* drop the ability bonuses, floor Int at 3 for every creature and grant SR HD+5 (cap 25) at every HD. When the floor gives a creature an Int score, clear `IsMindless` and the mindless immunity preset on the clone (keeping the vermin's other immunities only where the MM keeps them), and set the type of an animal or vermin base to Magical Beast with the augmented subtype, so neither `ApplyMindlessTrait` nor the vermin type check undoes it. Test with the celestial giant bee and a fiendish monstrous spider (Int 3, not mindless, affected by Sleep).
- **CRE-009** · **Med** — **Undead use 3/4 BAB.** `CreatureTypeProgression` gives Undead `BABProgression.Medium` and only the skeleton and zombie templates override it, so allip, wight, ghoul and other MM undead without class levels attack with too high a BAB (RAW: 1/2, poor). Their explicit BAB fields (wight BAB=2) are reference data only. Undead with real class levels (the lich, a wizard 11; the ghost and vampire, human fighter 5s as in MM p.117 and p.250) take the class BAB since CRE-004 (2026-10-08). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/CreatureTypeProgression.cs:97; Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/SkeletonTemplate.cs:113, ZombieTemplate.cs:107. *Fix:* change Undead to `BABProgression.Poor`.
- **CRE-010** · **Low** — **noble_djinni has an unregistered class.** It sets `CharacterClass = "Outsider"`, so every `ClassRegistry.GetClass` lookup for it logs "[ClassRegistry] Class not found: Outsider"; it is reachable through the dungeon encounter tables and the Ring of Djinni Calling. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs:56-61; Assets/Scripts/Character/Classes/ClassRegistry.cs:70-76. *Fix:* set `CharacterClass = "Warrior"` like the other MM monsters.
- **CRE-034** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Summon Monster I-IV lists deviate from the PHB.** SM II offers a fiendish Large monstrous scorpion where the PHB table (p.287) lists a Medium one (`monstrous_scorpion_medium` exists), and SM I-IV lack all PHB aquatic entries: celestial porpoise and fiendish octopus (I; `octopus` exists), fiendish squid and fiendish Medium shark (II), fiendish Large shark (IV; `large_shark` exists). The Summon Nature's Ally side of the aquatic gap is CRE-021. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Spell/Components/SummonMonsterLists.cs:154-177 (`GetSummonMonsterIIOptions`, line 168), 129-153, 219-275. *Fix:* point SM II at `monstrous_scorpion_medium`, add the entries whose NPC ids exist, and add the missing aquatic NPCs (with an underwater/no-water placement rule).
- **CRE-035** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Eight creatures have no Challenge Rating.** A read-only runtime pass over `NPCDatabase.AllNPCs` on 2026-10-04 found a blank `ChallengeRating` on monstrous_centipede_medium, skeleton_warrior, zombie_shambler, dire_bear, brown_bear, wolf, octopus and tiger; the static definitions of all but wolf (an alias clone of wolf_pack_hunter) set no CR. Several spawn from the DMG dungeon tables and the summon lists. For XP, `ExperienceCalculator.GetChallengeRating` falls back to the creature's level (at least 1), which is RAW only for NPCs with PC classes (DMG p.37); how EL and random-encounter math treat a blank CR was not checked (CR parsing: CRE-027). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabaseCustom.cs:184, 268, 655, 751, 788, 831 (wolf_pack_hunter, the wolf alias source); NPCDatabase_M.cs:373; NPCDatabase_O.cs:114; NPCDatabase.cs:246 (`RegisterSummonAlias("wolf", ...)`). *Fix:* add the MM CRs (and an `Init` warning for any blank CR).
- **CRE-036** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The MM blue slaad has pounce; the red slaad should.** `blue_slaad` sets `HasPounce = true`, but in the MM pounce belongs to the red slaad (MM p.229), not the blue (MM p.230). `red_slaad` also has it, which is correct. Found by the 2026-10-07 tactics inventory verification. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs:660-685; NPCDatabase_R.cs:110-132. *Fix:* remove `HasPounce` from `blue_slaad`.
- **CRE-075** · **Low** · Player-visible — **A controlled bombardier beetle is asked about its acid spray only when the triggering attack can wait for the answer.** Since batch 12 step 15 (2026-10-28, decision D1) the spray is a reaction (`GameManager.AttackedReactions.cs`): the attack hook asks the beetle's decider (`IAttackDecider.ChooseAttackedReaction`; a controlled beetle gets a Yes/No menu) when its caller runs it as a coroutine (the PC attack paths, the NPC ranged paths, the charge). Where the trigger resolves synchronously (an AoO or an NPC melee step, `RunAttackResolvedSync`; a hostile spell, at the end of the cast; damage from a foe's effect, in `DealDamage`; a maneuver, at the end of `CharacterController.ExecuteSpecialAttack`) a controlled beetle gets the AI's choice instead (spray when the cone catches more foes than allies), so a player's summoned beetle cannot decline a spray those triggers start. *Evidence:* `GameManager.FlushAttackedReactions`, `RunPendingAttackedReactions(canWait: false)`; `AttackResolution.CallerCanWait`. *Fix:* when the attack sequence executor (batch 12 step 17) and the maneuver core (step 19) yield, let them run the pending reactions as a coroutine.
- **CRE-043** · **Low** — **The NPC-by-level helpers give PC-class NPCs one CR too few and creatures too few feats.** DMG p.38 counts PC class levels in full toward CR (a dwarf fighter 10 is CR 10) and NPC class levels as one less (a dwarf warrior 10 is CR 9). `CRCalculator.GetStandardCR` returns level - 1 for PC classes and (level + 1) / 2 for NPC classes, and `TemplateData` hard-codes the same level - 1 values (Fighter 5 is CR 4, Fighter 20 is CR 19, so no template has CR 20). `CreatureClassEngine.FeatsFromTotalHD` and `ECLTracker.FeatsFromHD` return 1 + (HD - 1) / 3, so 3 HD gives 1 feat and 12 HD gives 4; PHB Table 3-2 gives a feat at 1st, 3rd, 6th, 9th and 12th level (1 + HD / 3). Nothing in play reaches this system (CRE-020); NPCTemplateSystemTests fails 6 assertions on it (2026-10-07). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/CreatureClass/CRCalculator.cs (`GetStandardCR`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/TemplateData.cs; Assets/Scripts/Character/CreatureClass/CreatureClassEngine.cs (`FeatsFromTotalHD`); Assets/Scripts/Character/CreatureClass/ECLTracker.cs (`FeatsFromHD`). *Fix:* CR = level for PC classes and level - 1 (CR 1/2 at 1st) for NPC classes, in the helper and the template data; feats = 1 + HD / 3.
- **CRE-044** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Undead and constructs stored with CON 0 spawn dead.** Spawned in Play mode with the scenario harness (HP mode Average), the allip (MM p.10; `BaseHitDieHP` 26, CON 0) and the wraith (MM p.257-258; CON 0) start at -10 HP, so they are dead before their first turn and the fight ends at once. Seen through the plain NPC ids (ad-hoc run 20261007-205402) and through the DMG dungeon spawner (soak seeds 24 and 36, run 20261007-204529, docs/TESTING.md 3.5). Cause (traced 2026-10-08 while fixing CHR-001): 15 definitions store the MM's missing Constitution as `CON = 0` instead of `CharacterStats.NO_SCORE` (allip, bodak, flesh golem, ghast, ghoul, greater shadow, ghost, lich, mohrg, mummy, shadow, spectre, vampire spawn, vampire, wraith; NPCDatabase_A/B/F/G/L/M/S/V/W), and `CharacterController.Init` runs `CheckAbilityScoreZeroEffects`, where `IsAbilityReducedToZero(CON)` treats a score of 0 as Constitution drained to 0 and kills the creature. The same CON 0 gives a -5 modifier to Fortitude saves and anything else that reads `GetModifier(CON)`. Hit points already treat CON 0 as no score (`ClassProgression.HitPointConstitutionModifier`, CHR-001), so the max HP is the MM total (allip 26, ghoul 13). Undead and constructs have no Constitution score (MM p.307, p.317). *Evidence:* the 15 definitions above; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (CheckAbilityScoreZeroEffects); Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs (IsAbilityReducedToZero, GetModifier). The six incorporeal entries (allip, greater shadow, ghost, shadow, spectre, wraith) store their missing Strength as `STR = 0` too, so a spawned shadow is also Helpless and cannot act (DMG p.290's Strength 0 rule read as drained Strength; found 2026-10-29 writing `rules/charge-incorporeal-over-grease`, run 20261029-082640: the shadow spawned at -10 HP, Helpless, speed 0, so the scenario uses a fighter made incorporeal). *Fix:* set those 15 entries to `CON = CharacterStats.NO_SCORE` and the six incorporeal ones to `STR = CharacterStats.NO_SCORE` (and audit every Undead and Construct entry), then test every no-CON creature (undead, constructs, incorporeal) in a harness scenario.
- **CRE-045** · **Low** · Player-visible — **NPC feat lists outside the level 1-8 scope are audited only for the attack-math feats.** On 2026-10-08 every definition the NPC database registers (the `NPCDatabase_*.cs` files, `NPCDatabaseCustom.cs` and the lycanthrope bases in `LycanthropeTemplate.cs`) was compared with its MM entry for Multiattack, Weapon Finesse, Power Attack, Weapon Focus and Improved Natural Attack and for the primary/secondary flags of its natural attacks, and the data was corrected (also the maneuver feats seen on the way: the blue slaad's Improved Bull Rush replaced by the MM's Dodge and Mobility, Improved Overrun added to the fire and frost giants, and the gibbering mouther's Dex 13 and natural armor +8); `Tests.Character.NpcFeatDataTests` fails when any definition lists Multiattack with fewer than three natural attacks (MM p.304), and the rules scenarios `rules/mm-natural-sequence-*` check two corrected sequences in Play mode. Since 2026-11-16 the MM stat-block audit has compared every feat of the 231 in-scope creatures (DMG tables levels 1-8, Summon Monster and Summon Nature's Ally I-IV) and filed each difference in that creature's own entry (CRE-081 to CRE-265; for example the xorns CRE-206, the gorgon CRE-123, the hellcat CRE-143, the behir CRE-106); it reported no feat difference for the babau, red slaad, phasm, shocker lizard and salamanders. Still open: (1) creatures outside that scope were not compared beyond the attack-math feats; mismatches seen in passing before the audit included Improved Initiative on the babau and the three xorns (not in the MM), Combat Reflexes on the green slaad (MM: Cleave, Improved Initiative), no Dodge and Mobility on the red slaad or the phasm, no Improved Initiative on the gorgon and the shocker lizard, Great Cleave on the purple worm, no Alertness on the salamanders (the badger's Agile was removed with CRE-003 on 2026-10-14). (2) The dragons' feats (`DragonData.cs`) are the data's own picks, since the MM gives no feat list per age; they were checked only against the Multiattack rule. `dire_wereboar` and `werewolf_afflicted` are custom builds with no MM block. (3) Stat-block mismatches seen in passing: green slaad Str 19 (MM p.230: 23), hellcat rake 1d6 (MM p.54: 1d8+3), and the octopus deals 1d3 with its arms and has no bite (MM p.276: arms 0 damage, bite +0 for 1d3); the behir has natural armor +8 (MM p.25: +11, AC 20); the skum has BAB +2, Con 15 and 13 hp (MM p.228: +1, Con 13, 2d8+2 = 11 hp). The 2026-11-16 audit re-compared the in-scope ones field by field (the hellcat in CRE-143, the octopus in CRE-247, the behir in CRE-106; it reported nothing for the skum); the green slaad is outside the scope and still unchecked. The dire tiger's printed rake +18 (MM p.65) is one below its BAB + Strength + size (+19, Weapon Focus (claw) left out); the data keeps the RAW formula and rolls +19. Corrected on 2026-10-14 with the grapple follow-up: the tiger's speed (40 ft., MM p.281), the dire tiger (rebuilt from MM p.65, it was a copy of the tiger), the behir's rake at half Strength, the leopard's grab trigger (its bite, MM p.274), the tiger's (claw or bite, MM p.281) and the skum's rake (a swimming rake, not a grapple rake, MM p.228). Weapon Focus and Improved Natural Attack are stored as the MM's 'Name (param)' strings and do nothing yet (CHR-008); Weapon Finesse is applied even when Strength is higher (CHR-073). The MM says salamanders have Multiattack although they lack three natural weapons (MM p.219), but its own tail-slap bonuses take the full -5; the data follows the feat's prerequisite (MM p.304) and leaves it out. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_*.cs (`Feats`, `NaturalAttacks`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs; Assets/Scripts/Tests/Character/NpcFeatDataTests.cs. *Fix:* compare the feats and stat blocks of creatures outside the level 1-8 scope with the MM when they come into scope, extending `NpcFeatDataTests` as entries are corrected.
- **CRE-047** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Several Improved Grab entries do not match the MM trigger attack.** `CharacterStats.IsImprovedGrabTriggerAttackName` (read by `GameManager.IsImprovedGrabTriggerAttack` and the AI's attack choice) matches the comma-separated `ImprovedGrabTriggerAttackName` entries (default "claw") against the attack name. Checked against the MM on 2026-10-08 while fixing CMB-126; the leopard (bite, MM p.274) and the tiger ("Claw,Bite", MM p.281) were corrected on 2026-10-14 with the grapple follow-up. Still wrong: the hellcat (MM p.55) grabs with the bite, but its entry names the claw; the couatl (bite, MM p.37) and the wyvern (talons, MM p.259) name no trigger, so the default "claw" never matches their attacks (`Bite`, `Talon`) and they never grab; the vampire has `HasImprovedGrab` on its slam although the MM vampire (p.250-251) has no Improved Grab. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_H.cs (`hellcat`), NPCDatabase_C.cs (`couatl`), NPCDatabase_W.cs (`wyvern`), NPCDatabase_V.cs (`vampire`); Assets/Scripts/Combat/Maneuvers/SupportActions.cs (`IsImprovedGrabTriggerAttack`). *Fix:* correct the trigger names (Bite for the hellcat and couatl, Talon for the wyvern) and remove the vampire's Improved Grab; the couatl's size maximum (Gargantuan) is already set.
- **CRE-048** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Five MM improved-grab creatures lack `HasImprovedGrab`, so they never grab.** Found on 2026-10-08 while reviewing CMB-126 (that audit covered only the flagged entries). The MM gives Improved Grab to: the celestial lion (bite, MM p.31; the plain `lion` has the flag), the average and flamebrother salamanders (tail slap, MM p.218, against a creature up to one size larger than the salamander: Large for the Medium average salamander, Medium for the Small flamebrother), the gray ooze (slam, MM p.202) and the ochre jelly (slam, MM p.203). The gray ooze and ochre jelly list "Improved Grab" only in `SpecialAbilities`, which is display text. Their constrict and rake follow-ups therefore never start either. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_C.cs (`celestial_lion`), NPCDatabase_A.cs (`average_salamander`), NPCDatabase_F.cs (`flamebrother_salamander`), NPCDatabase_G.cs (`gray_ooze`), NPCDatabase_O.cs (`ochre_jelly`). *Fix:* set `HasImprovedGrab` and `ImprovedGrabTriggerAttackName` (Bite; Tail Slap; Slam) on each, and `IsQuadruped` on the celestial lion (the Improved Grab drag limit's body plan, PHB p.162, CMB-163), and `ImprovedGrabMaxTargetSize` for the two salamanders (Large, Medium; a relative clause, see CMB-143); then check that their constrict data is wired.
- **CRE-049** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Hybrid-form werewolves keep Trip (Ex).** The game registers lycanthropes in hybrid form only (CRE-011), and `LycanthropeTemplate` copies the base animal's trip to that form (`lycan.HasTripAttack = animal.HasTrip`, with the comment that trip on the bite is kept for wolves), so the werewolf, the afflicted werewolf and the werewolf lord trip on a bite hit in hybrid form. MM p.175-176: a werewolf trips only in wolf form (the stat block lists curse of lycanthropy and trip for wolf form and only the curse for hybrid form), and a werewolf lord only in dire wolf form; in hybrid form it fights with weapons or claws. Since CMB-125 (2026-10-08) the trip follows only the bite (`TripTriggerAttackName` "Bite"), so a weapon or claw hit no longer trips. The template comment shows intent by an earlier agent, not an owner decision, and itself cites the RAW rule against it (MM p.176, the template's Special Attacks: the hybrid form gains none of the base animal's special attacks); the trip text is on MM p.174. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs (`HasTripAttack`, `TripTriggerAttackName` after the bite attack). *Fix:* ask the owner; for RAW, clear Trip (Ex) in hybrid form and give it back when animal forms exist (CRE-011). Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-050** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The giant praying mantis deviates from its MM stat block: two claw attacks, no hold bite, wrong Dex, Con, armor, hp and reach, and an invented Squeeze.** MM p.285: Large vermin, 4d8+8 (26 hp), AC 14 (-1 size, -1 Dex, +6 natural), Space/Reach 10 ft./5 ft., Str 19, Dex 8, Con 15; Attack claws +6 melee (1d8+4); Full Attack claws +6 melee (1d8+4) and bite +1 melee (1d6+2), so "claws" is one attack; Improved Grab on the claws, and once the hold is established it bites as a primary attack at its full +6. `giant_praying_mantis` differs: (1) `Claws` has `Count = 2`, so its full attack is claws, claws, bite (one extra 1d8+4 attack and a second chance to start Improved Grab); (2) no code gives the hold bite. Since CMB-149 (fixed 2026-10-11) the sequence stops once the grab holds (the mantis's one step on the BAB ladder is used, CMB-146), so the ordinary secondary bite no longer follows; RAW, the hold bite is one primary attack at +6 against the held creature that takes the place of that secondary bite, not an extra attack; (3) DEX 12 (MM 8), CON 17 (MM 15), `NaturalArmorBonus = 5` (MM +6), `BaseHitDieHP = 30` (MM 26 hp); (4) `IsTallCreature = true` gives it 10 ft. reach where the MM gives 5 ft. (a long Large creature); (5) `SpecialAbilities` carries "Improved Grab (claw attacks)" and "Squeeze (1d8+4 damage per round on grappled foe)"; the MM mantis has no squeeze, so that line is invented display text, not a spec (since CMB-148, fixed 2026-10-11, the hold's later Damage checks deal the claw's damage, MM p.310). The tactics inventory row (docs/systems/tactics/CREATURES_L1-4.md, `giant_praying_mantis`) already listed (3) and (5). The mantis decides seeds 6 and 49 of the soak reference (docs/TESTING.md 3.5), and its reach and hp change those fights as much as the second claw. *Seen in Play mode:* soak run 20261008-090434, seed 6 (`Logs/Scenarios/20261008-090434/soak_dmg-random-encounters__s6.jsonl`: "Claws 1" and "Claws 2" in one turn, then the secondary bite on the fighter, seq 113-114) and run 20261008-091027, seed 49; `Logs/` is machine-local, reproduce with `ScenarioHarness.QueueFresh("soak/*", "6,49", "")` at commit be4fd13. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs (`RegisterGiantPrayingMantis`). *Fix:* set `Count = 1` on `Claws`; DEX 8, CON 15, natural armor 6, 26 hp, clear `IsTallCreature`; replace the two text lines with "Improved Grab (claws; hold, then bite at +6)"; after a hold, make the bite one primary attack at +6 against the held creature in place of the secondary bite, through the Improved Grab aftermath (on top of the CMB-148 and CMB-149 fixes of 2026-10-11). Since 2026-10-14 a holder with one body part may not attack the held creature in any form (`CharacterController.IsBarredFromOrdinaryAttackOn`, delegated RAW reading, CMB-163); the mantis entry is specific text that overrides that bar for the hold bite, so the bite needs an exemption there (and the holding claws should make no other attack, CMB-171); check other definitions whose MM entry lists a plural attack name as a single attack ("Claws", "Tentacles") before changing counts.
- **CRE-051** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Undead, constructs and swarms are not destroyed at 0 hit points; they become disabled, dying or stable like living creatures.** MM p.317 (undead) and p.307 (constructs): a creature of either type is destroyed as soon as it drops to 0 hit points or less. MM p.316 (swarm subtype): a swarm reduced to 0 hit points or lower breaks up and is never staggered or dying; `CharacterStats.IsSwarm` marks one (rat, bat and spider swarms are in the level 1-8 tables). Since the combat-end change of 2026-10-08 (CORE-011) a disabled creature counts as still in the fight, so a swarm, zombie or construct at exactly 0 HP keeps the fight going where the old HP <= 0 test ended it. `CharacterController.DetermineStateFromHPTransition` reads only the hit point total, Diehard and nonlethal damage, never `CharacterStats.CreatureType`, and nothing else in the damage path checks the type. So a zombie or skeleton at exactly 0 HP is Disabled and keeps acting: it can take its single action, make AoOs, and since the CMB-136 ruling of 2026-10-08 trip back after a failed trip (that ruling lets a disabled defender counter-trip; the rule is correct for living creatures). Between -1 and -9 HP it is Dying and can become Stable, instead of being destroyed. Since batch 12 step 3 (D5) such a creature also goes on with a charge it began: in the batch 12 midpoint soak (2026-10-28, run 20261028-142524, seed 7, seq 117-160) a skeleton warrior that a cleric's AoO drops to exactly 0 HP finishes its charge and drops the party wizard (CMB-176). The fights still end, because a dying or stable creature counts as out of the fight, so the main effect is the exactly-0 window and wrong state labels (and anything that reads a stable or dying undead, such as healing or coup de grace). *Seen in Play mode:* soak batch 20261008-130416 (batch 4 full regression, commit 4e51e1a). Seed 10, run 20261008-130539 (`Logs/Scenarios/20261008-130539/soak_dmg-random-encounters__s10.jsonl`): `zombie_human_commoner` goes Disabled at 0 HP (seq 107). On the fighter's turn it makes the trip initiation AoO (seq 126) and a counter-trip (seq 131), then goes Dying at -2 (seq 146) and ends the fight Dying. Seed 7, same run: `skeleton_warrior` goes Dying and then Stable at -1. Seed 42, run 20261008-131311: `zombie_shambler` ends Dying at -4. `Logs/` is machine-local; to reproduce, run `ScenarioHarness.QueueFresh("soak/*", "7,10,42", "")` at commit 4e51e1a. Lay on Hands on an undead creature (CHR-020, 2026-10-14) spends exactly its remaining hit points (`LayOnHandsRules.Resolve`), and the AI picks an undead foe the pool can bring to 0 (`AIProfile.DefaultLayOnHandsTarget`), both assuming destruction at 0 hit points; until this fix the zombie of `rules/paladin-lay-on-hands-undead` is left disabled at 0 and keeps acting. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (`DetermineStateFromHPTransition`, `SetHPState`). *Fix:* when the creature type is undead or construct, or `IsSwarm` is set, and HP falls to 0 or below, go straight to `HPState.Dead` (so the combat-end predicate needs no special case), logged as destroyed. Combat end no longer has a regeneration or fast-healing exception (CORE-034, fixed 2026-10-08: `CombatEndRules.IsOutOfFight`); check the allip spawn bug (CRE-044) at the same time, and add a suite case (in `NonlethalDamageTests` or a new Play-mode HP-state suite) and a rules scenario. The soak cannot catch this bug by itself: `ScenarioChecks.HpStateProblem` (Assets/Scripts/Tests/Scenarios/ScenarioChecks.cs) accepts Disabled, Dying and Stable at HP 0 or below for every creature type, so the soak verdicts stay Pass or Known while the bug is live and nothing turns XPASS when it is fixed. With the fix, add an undead-or-construct check to `HpStateProblem` (HP 0 or below, type undead or construct, and state not Dead is a violation of the hp-bounds invariant) and, if it lands before the fix, a global waiver on CRE-051 in `KnownIssueWaivers.cs`, so the soak reports it as Known and then as an unused waiver once the fix lands.
- **CRE-052** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Eight item ids in NPC equipment do not exist, so those creatures spawn without the item.** `dwarven_waraxe`, `hand_crossbow`, `heavy_pick`, `heavy_steel_shield`, `heavy_wooden_shield`, `light_crossbow`, `light_steel_shield` and `repeating_light_crossbow` are not `ItemDatabase` ids (the real ids are for example `shield_heavy_steel`, `shield_light_wooden`; checked with `ItemDatabase.GetItem` in the editor on 2026-10-08). `InitializeNPCFromDefinition` logs "Item not found" and goes on, so the hobgoblin (MM p.153) has no shield, the lizardfolk (MM p.169) no shield, and drow, duergar, dwarf_warrior, svirfneblin, derro and halfling_warrior lose a weapon or shield. Separate from ITM-004 (valid ids in the MainHand, OffHand or Ranged slots, which DirectEquip dropped until 2026-10-08). Found while fixing CHR-072. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_D.cs, _H.cs, _L.cs, _S.cs (the drow, duergar, dwarf_warrior, derro, halfling_warrior, hobgoblin, lizardfolk and svirfneblin entries); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs (InitializeNPCFromDefinition, "Item not found"). *Fix:* use the real ids (or `ItemIDs` constants), add the missing items (dwarven waraxe, heavy pick, hand and repeating crossbows) to `ItemDatabase`, and fail loudly on an unknown id. A classed humanoid gets no proficiency from what it carries (CHR-072), so list racial exotic weapons (the dwarf's waraxe through weapon familiarity, the drow's hand crossbow) in `EntryWeaponIds` when the items exist.
- **CRE-054** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Skeletons and zombies lose the base creature's class weapon proficiencies.** MM p.226 (skeleton) and p.266 (zombie): the creature keeps the base creature's weapon proficiencies. `SkeletonTemplate` and `ZombieTemplate` drop the class (`CharacterClass = null`, marked a racial-HD stand-in for CHR-072), so a skeleton or zombie keeps only the weapons its definition carries and the undead type's simple weapons (MM p.317): the skeleton of a human warrior or a hobgoblin is not proficient with a martial weapon it does not carry. No database skeleton or zombie carries a weapon it is not proficient with (checked in edit mode on 2026-10-08), so this shows only on a later weapon swap or pickup. Not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/SkeletonTemplate.cs, ZombieTemplate.cs (`CharacterClass = null`, `ClassLevelsAreRacialHitDice = true`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/CreatureProficiency.cs. *Fix:* record the base creature's weapon proficiency (its class and type grants) on the template result, for example as `EntryWeaponIds` or a weapon-only class marker, and apply it in `CreatureProficiency.ApplyFromDefinition`; then assert martial weapons for `skeleton_warrior` in `NpcProficiencyTests`.
- **CRE-055** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Lycanthrope base creatures have no class, although their ids name one.** `LycanthropeTemplate`'s base definitions (`base_human_warrior_1`, `base_human_fighter_4`, `base_human_fighter_10`, `base_human_rogue_1`, `base_human_barbarian_1`, `base_human_barbarian_1b`, `base_human_commoner_1`, `base_human_commoner_afflicted`) set no `CharacterClass`. Before CHR-072 the empty class fell back to Fighter and so granted every weapon and armor proficiency; since 2026-10-08 an empty class is a racial-HD stand-in, so these lycanthropes get the humanoid and shapechanger proficiencies (simple weapons, the weapons they carry, the armor they wear and lighter, shields; MM p.310, p.314) instead of their class's. A werewolf lord (human fighter 10) is therefore not proficient with a martial weapon it does not carry or with heavier armor than it wears, and a commoner-based one gains all simple weapons. Their carried gear keeps its proficiency, so their attacks today do not change. Not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs (the base definitions in `Werewolf`, `WerewolfLord` and the others). *Fix:* give each base definition the class its id names (Warrior, Fighter, Rogue, Barbarian, Commoner) with `ClassLevelsAreRacialHitDice = false`, after checking the side effects of `HasClass` (rage, sneak attack, fighter bonus feats, CRE-024's `IsFighter` readers).
- **CRE-058** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Class levels added to a creature give no feats, skill points, spells or gear, and an NPC definition holds one class.** Since CRE-004 (2026-10-08) a spawned creature's BAB and base saves are its racial Hit Dice by the creature type's progressions (or the entry's overrides) plus its real class levels by the class tables, in the same `CharacterStats` sums a PC uses, and a humanoid with 1 HD or less exchanges its Hit Die for the class levels (MM p.290, `CreatureClassEngine.ClassLevelsReplaceHitDice`). Since 2026-10-14 the class also sets the ability scores and the maximized Hit Die (delegated RAW readings, docs/systems/RULES_COVERAGE.md 4.1; `ClassAdvancement`): a DMG table row labelled "NPC" is an elite DMG chapter 4 NPC (DMG p.78 step 4, p.110): the elite array arranged as the class's DMG chapter 4 table prints it with that table's increases by Hit Dice, plus the racial adjustments (`StatArrayApplier`), and the maximum on its first Hit Die, the first racial die when the base keeps racial Hit Dice (DMG p.172-173); a PC class added outside those rows gets the elite array, with the maximum only on a replaced humanoid's first die; an NPC class gets the nonelite array and averages every die (a 1-HD humanoid keeps its MM warrior scores); a row naming an MM sample keeps the sample when the base is the sample, and otherwise is built by the MM p.294 rule the sample follows, keeping the base's alignment (`ClassAdvancement.PrintedSample`). What `CreatureClassEngine.ApplyClassToDefinition` (DMG-table "Race Class N" encounters, `QuickSpawnSystem`) still leaves out: the feats of the added Hit Dice (PHB Table 3-2, by total HD, plus class bonus feats; a replaced humanoid keeps its MM feat), skill points (NPC skills are not modelled, CHR-067), class spells and spell slots (an adept added this way cannot cast, CHR-024), class gear by level (DMG ch.4), and ability scores for a base that already has real class levels (kept) or a 1-HD humanoid with no race data (kept, a warning is logged). Save DCs stored on a definition (poison, stench) are not recomputed from a rebuilt score. `NPCDefinition` holds one `CharacterClass` and `Level`, so a definition with real class levels that gets another class counts the old levels as racial Hit Dice (a warning is logged; no data path does this today, since the DMG table bases are monsters or 1-HD warriors and commoners, which are replaced). Lycanthrope hybrids keep a fixed `BaseAttackBonusOverride` (the base's BAB field plus the animal's) and good Fortitude and Reflex with poor Will over all Hit Dice, not the base creature's values plus the animal's (MM p.175; their bases have no class, CRE-055). The CR of a replaced humanoid is still computed from its old Hit Die (CRE-043). Fixed BAB overrides remain on the bat, rat and spider swarms (equal to their type progression today) and the lycanthropes; the goblins take their BAB from their warrior level since 2026-10-08. Save progressions other than the type default are entry overrides: the gnoll and troglodyte (good Fortitude) and the dire animals (good Will, MM p.62-65) are set; the other animals' good saves (MM p.306 lets an animal differ from the type's good Fortitude and Reflex) are not audited against their MM stat blocks. A DMG table row that names the class level of a class-level sample (DMG p.97 "ghost/vampire, 5th-level human fighter", the MM p.117 and p.250 fighter 5s) adds only the missing levels (`CreatureClassEngine.ApplyEncounterClassLevel`), so those rows spawn the sample; the ghost and vampire templates themselves are still fixed stat blocks (CREATURES.md section 4, ENCOUNTERS.md plan item 7). The live DMG "NPC" rows that spawn today are the hobgoblin fighter 5, goblin rogue 5, lizardfolk druid 5 and troglodyte cleric 5 (dungeon levels 5-8; the human and kobold rows have no base, ENCOUNTERS.md section 3.5). The hard-coded fallback table (used when the CSV fails to load) spawns its "5th-level human monk NPC" and paladin rows as the hand-built `human_monk_5` and `human_paladin_5`, which are the DMG p.118 samples since 2026-10-14 (elite scores, 31 and 37 hp); its 3rd- and 7th-level human monk and paladin rows (`human_monk_3`/`_7`, `human_paladin_3`/`_7`) are not audited against the DMG chapter 4 tables, and none of the four carries the sample's gear. Checked by `Tests.Character.CreatureProgressionTests` (elite rows, monster class levels, class-legal alignments of every class row) and `rules/class-progression`, `rules/creature-progression`; not verified in Play mode beyond those. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/CreatureClass/CreatureClassEngine.cs (`ApplyClassToDefinition`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs (`NPCDefinition.CharacterClass`, `Level`, `ResolveRacialHitDice`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs (`Apply`). *Fix:* the creature factory of docs/systems/PC_NPC_PARITY.md step 11: a Hit Dice list on the definition (racial blocks by type, the lycanthrope's animal block, class levels), feats by total HD, and class features and spells through the PC level-up path.
- **CRE-059** · **Low** · Player-visible — **evil_acolyte_test spawns with no prepared spells.** Its definition prepares Daze four times, but Daze is not a cleric spell (PHB p.217: Brd 0, Sor/Wiz 0), so `SpellcastingComponent.ApplyPreparedSpellSlotIds` rejects every entry ("Ignored invalid prepared spell") and all nine of its slots stay empty: it can cast nothing, and the `protection_from_evil_test` preset never gets its evil caster's Daze. Seen in Play mode on 2026-10-09 (a scenario probe listed every slot empty). *Evidence:* `evil_acolyte_test` in `Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabaseCustom.cs`; `ApplyPreparedSpellSlotIds` and `PrepareSpellInSlot` in `Assets/Scripts/Spell/Components/SpellcastingComponent.cs`. *Fix:* prepare cleric orisons and 1st- and 2nd-level spells (for example a mind-affecting cleric spell such as Command for the preset's purpose), and warn when an NPC definition prepares a spell its class cannot cast.
- **CRE-060** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Swarm distraction ignores whether the creature is vulnerable to the swarm.** MM p.316 (Swarm subtype): distraction affects a living creature vulnerable to the swarm's damage; a creature is not vulnerable when it is immune to that damage, incorporeal, or its damage reduction stops the swarm's maximum damage. `AIService.ApplySwarmDamageToOccupants` asks every surviving creature on the swarm's square for the Fortitude save, undead and constructs included. *Evidence:* `Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs` (`ApplySwarmDamageToOccupants`). *Fix:* decide vulnerability once per creature (immunity to the swarm's damage type, incorporeal, or the best applicable damage reduction or resistance at least the swarm's maximum) and skip distraction for those and for nonliving creatures.
- **CRE-061** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The thoqqua's slam carries a flat +6 instead of its 2d6 fire heat.** MM p.242: slam 1d6+3 plus 2d6 fire. The thoqqua's `NaturalAttackDefinition` sets `BonusDamage = 6`, which looks like the heat flattened into a number. Since CMB-003 (2026-10-09) no attack path adds a natural attack's `BonusDamage` (the sunder path did until then); only the AI's natural attack damage estimate (`CharacterController` near the `GetNaturalAttackDamageBonus(natural) + natural.BonusDamage` line) still reads it, so the heat is missing from its hits and the AI overrates the slam by 6. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_T.cs (thoqqua slam); Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (AI natural attack damage estimate). *Fix:* model the heat as a 2d6 fire on-hit rider and drop `BonusDamage` (and the field, if nothing else needs it). Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-062** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The human paladins and the Ranger 4 test archers spawn with no spells.** `GameManager.InitializeNPCFromDefinition` adds and initializes a `SpellcastingComponent` only when the definition sets `KnownSpellIds` or `PreparedSpellSlotIds`. human_paladin (Paladin 5), the DMG random-encounter paladins human_paladin_5 and human_paladin_7 (a paladin casts from 4th level, PHB p.44; at 4th and 5th level only bonus spells from Wisdom) and the Ranger 4 test archers ranged_test_archer_ne and ranged_test_archer_north (a ranger casts from 4th level, PHB p.48, also only bonus spells at 4th) set neither; human_paladin_3 and the lower-level rangers have no spells by RAW. Since 2026-10-14 the spawn logs a warning for every creature whose class levels grant spells and whose definition lists none (`GameManager.NpcClassGrantsSpells`). human_cleric, the first part of this entry, was fixed on 2026-10-14: it has the DMG p.114 sample 5th-level cleric's domains (Healing, Protection), alignment (neutral good) and prepared spells (5/5/4/3) through the new `NPCDefinition.ClericDomains` and the shared cleric setup of `SpellcastingComponent` (checked by `ClericSpellcastingRulesTests` and `rules/prayer-cast` in Play mode, TST-040). Same mechanism as ENC-021 for DMG class-levelled spawns. *Evidence:* the paladin and archer entries in Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabaseCustom.cs and NPCDatabase_P.cs; `shouldInitSpellcasting` in Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs. *Fix:* give these definitions prepared spell lists that follow their class tables and Wisdom bonus spells (the partial-caster path of `SpellcastingComponent.InitPartialCaster` needs checking with a definition list).
- **CRE-063** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The bodak and vampire feat lists differ from the MM.** Found by the CRE-041 Toughness audit on 2026-10-14. MM p.28: the bodak has Alertness, Dodge, Improved Initiative and Weapon Focus (slam); `bodak` lists Dodge, Improved Initiative and Toughness. MM p.250-251: the vampire 5th-level human fighter sample has no Toughness; `vampire` lists Alertness, Combat Reflexes, Dodge, Improved Initiative, Lightning Reflexes and Toughness. Both hit point totals (58, 32) match the MM, which leaves Toughness out, so since CRE-041 the spawn keeps them right; the feats themselves still change the attack (Weapon Focus missing) and the AI's choices. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs (`bodak`), NPCDatabase_V.cs (`vampire`); `Tests.Character.CreatureProgressionTests.ToughnessTotals`. *Fix:* set both feat lists to the MM (and, for the vampire, its fighter bonus feats), then drop both from the Toughness table of that test. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-064** · **Low** — **The template registry applies acquired templates before inherited ones and lets celestial and fiendish onto undead.** Found with the CRE-001 fix on 2026-10-14. MM p.293: several templates are applied one at a time, inherited templates (celestial, fiendish; MM p.291) before acquired ones (skeleton, zombie). The celestial and fiendish templates take a corporeal aberration, animal, dragon, fey, giant, humanoid, magical beast, monstrous humanoid, plant or vermin (MM p.31, p.108), the skeleton and zombie templates any corporeal creature other than an undead (MM p.225, p.265). `CreatureTemplateRegistry.ApplyTemplatesClone` sorts by `ApplicationOrder` (undead 10, lycanthrope 15, outsider 20), so a request for "skeleton" and "fiendish" makes a skeleton first and then adds the fiendish template to an undead; `OutsiderTemplateBase.CanApplyTo` checks only the alignment and the skeleton and zombie factories never check the base's type. No database entry, summon option or dungeon row requests such a pair today (dungeon rows parse no celestial or fiendish prefix, ENC-022), so only tests reach it (`GrappleDamageRulesTests` builds a fiendish skeleton worg). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/CreatureTemplateFramework.cs (`ICreatureTemplate.ApplicationOrder`, `ApplyTemplatesClone`, `OutsiderTemplateBase.CanApplyTo`). *Fix:* apply inherited templates first (a lycanthrope by how it was gained) and refuse a base of the wrong type, as CRE-057 does for alignment.
- **CRE-065** · **Med** · Player-visible — **A giant bee does not die after a successful sting.** MM p.284: a giant bee that stings a creature successfully pulls away, leaves its stinger in it and dies. Nothing models this: the `giant_bee` sting is an ordinary natural attack with `PoisonOnHitId = "giant_bee_poison"`, resolved by the shared natural-attack hit path (`CharacterController`, where `naturalAttack.PoisonOnHitId` is read), so a bee hits and poisons every round. Since CRE-003 (2026-10-14) the real MM bee is in play: the Summon Monster II celestial giant bee (PHB p.287) of a PC or an NPC caster stings for the whole spell duration instead of once, and a random-encounter bee does the same. The text is only in its `Description` and in docs/systems/tactics/SUMMONS.md. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs (`RegisterGiantBee`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (the natural-attack poison on hit). *Fix:* a data flag on `NaturalAttackDefinition`, for example `DiesAfterSuccessfulHit`, set on the bee's sting and read in the shared natural-attack hit path both sides use: after a hit (after the damage and poison are applied) the attacker dies (a summon then despawns through `HandleSummonDeathCleanup`), and the AI values the sting as a one-shot attack. Test it with a rules scenario. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-066** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Creatures with less than 1 Hit Die are stored as 1 HD.** The MM gives fractional Hit Dice to several Tiny and Diminutive creatures (for example the bat, MM p.268, the raven, MM p.278, and the Tiny viper, MM p.280, each 1/4 d8 for 1 hp). `NPCDefinition.HitDice` is an int, so the two such entries in the database, `raven` and `viper_tiny`, are 1 HD creatures; their hit points match the MM, but every reader of `CharacterStats.GetHitDice()` counts a full die: the HD pools of Sleep and Deep Slumber (PHB p.280, p.217) charge a raven 1 HD instead of 1/4, Color Spray's HD bands and HD-sorted spells such as Circle of Death read 1, and so do the template SR of HD+5 (CRE-008) and the CR and feat formulas. Since CRE-003 (2026-10-14) `raven` is the MM raven, reachable as the Summon Monster I fiendish raven (PHB p.287) and in the random-encounter pool. The MM's own rule treats a creature with less than 1 HD as having 1 HD only for skill points and maximum skill ranks (MM p.301). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs (`NPCDefinition.HitDice`), NPCDatabase_R.cs (`raven`), NPCDatabase_V.cs (`viper_tiny`); Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager.SpellCasting.cs (the Sleep and Deep Slumber HD pools, `ResolveColorSpraySpell`). *Fix:* store the fraction (for example a Hit Dice fraction beside `HitDice`), have the HD pools and HD bands read it, and keep 1 HD for skills and ranks; check the bat and other fractional-HD MM creatures when they are added. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-068** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The brown bear lacks Improved Grab, and the polar bear is missing.** MM p.269: the brown bear and the polar bear both have Improved Grab (Ex) on a claw hit. `brown_bear` (also a Summon Nature's Ally IV choice) has two claws and a bite but no `HasImprovedGrab`, `ImprovedGrabTriggerAttackName` or `IsQuadruped`, so it never grabs, and there is no polar bear entry. Once the bear grabs, its Improved Grab drag limit must use the quadruped carrying multipliers (PHB p.162; delegated RAW reading 2026-10-14, CMB-163), which it gets only if `IsQuadruped` is set. Found by grep while reviewing CMB-163 on 2026-10-14; not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabaseCustom.cs (`brown_bear`). *Fix:* set `HasImprovedGrab = true`, `ImprovedGrabTriggerAttackName = "Claw"` and `IsQuadruped = true` on `brown_bear`; add a `polar_bear` entry from MM p.269 with the same three flags.
- **CRE-067** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The giant worker ant lists an acid sting it does not have, and the giant soldier ant is missing.** MM p.284 (Giant Ant): the worker has a bite and Improved Grab only; the acid sting belongs to the soldier, which may sting a creature it has grabbed each round (+3 attack, 1d4+1 piercing plus 1d4 acid). `giant_worker_ant` carries the display line "Acid Sting: 1d4+1 acid (workers have reduced sting)" in `SpecialAbilities`, invented text with no mechanics behind it, and no `giant_soldier_ant` entry exists. Found while checking the CMB-163 attack bar on 2026-10-14 (the soldier's sting is one of the creature entries that may attack its held opponent); not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs (`giant_worker_ant`). *Fix:* drop the line from the worker; add the soldier from its MM stat block, with its sting allowed against the creature it holds (an exemption to `IsBarredFromOrdinaryAttackOn`, as for the mantis hold bite, CRE-050).
- **CRE-069** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The DMG row "ogre barbarian, 4th level" spawns the ogre plus barbarian levels built by the MM p.294 rule, not the MM ogre barbarian itself.** Found with the CRE-058 readings on 2026-10-14. DMG p.81 (dungeon level 7) names the MM p.199 sample, which DMG p.78 step 4 sends to the MM: 4d8+19 plus 4d12+16 = 79 hp, Str 26, Dex 11, Con 18, Int 8, Wis 10, Cha 4 (the elite array arranged the MM's own way, the ogre's adjustments and one increase at 8 HD), and its own feats and gear. No `ogre_barbarian` definition exists, so the row (`ClassAdvancement.PrintedSample`) clones the ogre and adds barbarian 4 by the rule the sample itself follows (delegated RAW reading 2026-10-14, CRE-058): the barbarian table's elite arrangement plus the ogre's adjustments and the 8-HD increase on Con, every die averaged. That gives Con 18 and the printed 79 hp, but Str 25, Dex 12, Int 6, Wis 12 (the DMG barbarian arrangement, not the MM's), and the ogre's feats and gear instead of the sample's. The "1 half-dragon 4th-level fighter" row (DMG p.81, the MM p.146 sample, 43 hp) does not spawn at all (no human base, no half-dragon template; ENC-005). Checked by `CreatureProgressionTests.TestMonsterClassAdvancement`; not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/StreamingAssets/dungeon_encounters.csv (7,60-61); Assets/Scripts/Encounters/DungeonEncounterTableData.cs (hard-coded table 7); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_O.cs (`ogre`). *Fix:* register the MM ogre barbarian sample (and the half-dragon fighter once a human base and the template exist) and map the rows to them; `ApplyEncounterClassLevel` then adds no levels, as for the ghost and vampire.

- **CRE-070** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The invisible stalker is an outsider in the data, not an elemental.** MM p.160: Large Elemental (Air, Extraplanar), 8d8+16, Base Attack/Grapple +6/+14, Fort +4, Ref +10, Will +4 (PHB Table 3-14 uses it as its example elemental). `invisible_stalker` has `CreatureType = "Outsider"` and an "Outsider" tag, so it gets the outsider progressions (full BAB +8, all good saves) instead of the elemental's three-quarters BAB and the air elemental's good Reflex, and a ranger with the Elemental favored enemy gets no bonus against it while one with Outsider (Air) does (CHR-020). The lizardfolk's wrong "Aquatic" tag (MM p.169: Humanoid (Reptilian)) was removed with CHR-020 on 2026-10-14. Found reviewing CHR-020; not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_I.cs (`invisible_stalker`). *Fix:* set `CreatureType = "Elemental"` and the tags Elemental, Air, Extraplanar, check the spawned BAB (+6) and saves (good Reflex for the Air subtype, as the air elementals get them) against MM p.160 and the elemental traits, and add a `RangerClassRulesTests` matching case.
- **CRE-071** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The deinonychus has natural armor +4; the MM gives +5.** MM p.60 (pdf p.61): Medium Animal, AC 17 (+2 Dex, +5 natural). `deinonychus` has `NaturalArmorBonus = 4`, so its AC is 16, and a druid in its wild shape form (CHR-020; the AI's usual pick at druid 5) gets +4 natural armor instead of +5. Its attacks, Hit Dice, hit points and speed match the MM. Found while checking the wild shape forms on 2026-10-14; not checked against every other animal entry. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_D.cs (`RegisterDeinonychus`). *Fix:* set `NaturalArmorBonus = 5`, and compare the natural armor of the other animal entries with their MM AC lines.

- **CRE-072** · **Low** · Player-visible — **NPCs never get the priced components their prepared spells need.** Since batch 11 step 17 (decision D3, 2026-10-15) every caster needs its components and an NPC spawns with the cheap kit (`SpellComponentKit`) but no `CharacterStats.ComponentGold` and no priced component items, so a prepared spell with a priced material component or focus (the lich's Stoneskin and Circle of Death; any NPC Identify, Restoration, Augury, Shield Other) is refused at Legality and the AI leaves it out. RAW, the DM spends an NPC's gear value (DMG p.127, Table 4-23) on its equipment (DMG p.110-112 for the class NPCs), and an MM caster's possessions are the DM's to choose; decision D3 reads that a priced component comes from that wealth or as treasure. `SpellComponentRulesTests`' audit logs the spells left out. PCs, meanwhile, pay such components from 1,000 gp of component gold no rule gives (ITM-029; PC_NPC_PARITY section 4). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs (`InitializeNPCFromDefinition`: `ComponentGold = 0`); Assets/Scripts/Spell/Casting/SpellComponentKit.cs; Assets/Scripts/Tests/Magic/SpellComponentRulesTests.cs (`TestNpcAudit`). *Fix:* at spawn, buy the priced components (and priced foci) of the NPC's prepared spells from its gear wealth as the DMG budgets it, or place them in its treasure, as items the cast spends; settle with ITM-029 how a PC pays.
- **CRE-074** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The ankheg's spit leaves its bite acid on, and the digester has no acid stream.** Found auditing the breath weapons against the MM in batch 12 step 6 (2026-10-28). MM p.15: one spit depletes the ankheg's acid supply for 6 hours, during which it cannot spit acid or deal acid damage; the ankheg's bite keeps its 1d4 acid (`NaturalAttackDefinition.BonusElementalDamage*`, NPCDatabase_A.cs) after the spit (`BreathWeaponDefinition.RechargeRounds` 3600). MM p.59: besides the 20-ft cone, a digester can spray a concentrated 8d8 stream at one target within 5 feet (Reflex DC 17 half, the same 1d4-round recharge); only the cone is modelled. Both are latent while only Dragon-profile creatures breathe (AI-033). Not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_A.cs (ankheg), NPCDatabase_D.cs (digester); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCTurns.cs (`NPCExecuteBreathWeaponForAI`). *Fix:* let a breath definition switch off the creature's acid bonus damage until it recharges, and give the digester a second, single-target mode that shares the recharge.
- **CRE-076** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Gaze attacks run as auras with none of the gaze rules, and the spirit naga's charming gaze frightens.** The gazes are `AuraAbilityDefinition`s (the basilisk's and medusa's Petrifying Gaze, the chain devil's Unnerving Gaze, the spirit naga's Charming Gaze, the Confusing Gaze in `NPCDatabase_U`), resolved by `GameManager.ProcessTurnStartAura` at the start of the bearer's acting turn (since batch 13 step 19, D13, for every bearer, compelled turns included): every opponent in range saves once per bearer turn. RAW (MM p.309-310, Gaze): each opponent in range saves at the start of its own turn, and only one that looks at the creature is at risk; an opponent may avert its eyes (50% each round of needing no save, the gazer gaining concealment against it) or shut them (no save, total concealment), so a blind creature is safe; the gazer can also actively gaze at one target as an attack action; the gazer's allies count as averting their eyes and may be affected; a creature is immune to the gaze of its own kind. None of that is modelled. And the spirit naga's gaze (`NPCDatabase_N`) has `EffectType = AuraEffectType.Fear`, which `AuraEffectToCondition` maps to Frightened, where MM p.193 makes it a charm person effect (and gives Will DC 19; the aura's `SaveDC` is 17). Read in code (2026-10-29); not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Combat/Special/GameManager.MonsterTriggers.cs (`ProcessTurnStartAura`); Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs (`AuraEffectToCondition`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_{B,C,M,N,U}.cs (the `Gaze` auras). *Fix:* batch 16: a gaze rule beside the aura (saves at each opponent's turn start, the looking test, avert and shut-eyes choices for both sides, the active gaze as an attack action) and the naga's gaze as a charm. Found mapping batch 13 (2026-10-29).
- **CRE-077** · **Low** · Player-visible — **A breath weapon can deal only damage: its failed-save effect is not modelled.** `BreathWeaponDefinition` (NPCDatabase.cs ~:291) has shape, range, damage dice, bonus and type, save DC and type, `SaveNegates`, recharge and uses per day, and no field for a condition on a failed save, so `NPCExecuteBreathWeaponForAI` can only deal damage. The gorgon's breath (`NPCDatabase_G`, MM p.138: a cone that turns creatures to stone on a failed Fortitude save, up to five times per day) is defined with no damage dice and so does nothing at all when breathed. (The metallic dragons' gases are a separate `SecondaryBreathWeaponDefinition` with effect types, never used, AI-008.) Fields checked 2026-10-29; not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs (`BreathWeaponDefinition`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs (the gorgon's breath, `DamageDice = 0`); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCTurns.cs (`NPCExecuteBreathWeaponForAI`, "a breath with no damage dice deals none"). *Fix:* batch 16: a failed-save effect on the breath definition (condition and duration), applied by the shared breath executor of batch 13 step 18. Found mapping batch 13 (2026-10-29).
- **CRE-078** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Engulf follows one simplified rule for every engulfer: a Reflex save or be engulfed, against one adjacent creature.** Since batch 13 step 18 (2026-10-30, D11) engulf is a shared catalog entry (`EngulfActionExecutor`, `AIService.ExecuteEngulfCore`) that any creature with an `EngulfDefinition` takes, the PC's Monster Abilities button included: one adjacent creature of Large size or smaller (the size limit added in the step's review) makes a Reflex save or is engulfed, as a standard action. Only part of that is the gelatinous cube's rule (MM p.202): the cube engulfs by moving over its opponents, as many as it covers; an opponent may make an attack of opportunity against it in place of the save; one that saves is pushed back or aside; the cube makes no slam in a round it engulfs. None of these is modelled. The other engulfers have different rules: the cloaker (`NPCDatabase_C`, MM p.36) wraps a Medium or smaller creature as a standard action by a grapple attempt that provokes no attack of opportunity, with no Reflex save; the gibbering mouther (`NPCDatabase_G`, MM p.126) swallows a Medium or smaller opponent it has grappled; the ochre jelly (`NPCDatabase_O`, MM p.202-203) has no engulf at all (acid, constrict and improved grab). The engulfed condition's lasting Helpless state is CMB-024. Checked against the data and the MM on 2026-10-30; not verified in Play mode beyond `rules/engulf-ui` and `rules/engulf-large-adjacent`. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Combat/Actions/Executors/MonsterAbilityExecutors.cs (`EngulfActionExecutor`); Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs (`ExecuteEngulfCore`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_C.cs, NPCDatabase_G.cs, NPCDatabase_O.cs (the `Engulf` definitions). *Fix:* per creature: the cube's engulf as a move over opponents with the AoO-or-save choice, the push aside and the no-slam round; the cloaker's and the mouther's as their own grapple-based abilities; the ochre jelly's definition removed. Found by the batch 13 step 18 review (2026-10-30).
- **CRE-079** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The allip's babble, the cloaker's moan, the shadow mastiff's bay, the mummy's despair and the krenshar's scare run as one generic turn-start aura with simplified effects.** Since batch 13 step 19 (2026-10-30, decision D13) every `AuraAbilityDefinition` fires at the start of its bearer's acting turn (`GameManager.ProcessTurnStartAura`), which fits gibbering (MM p.126) but not these two. The allip (`NPCDatabase_A`, "Babble") applies Fascinated for a fixed 3 rounds to opponents only; MM p.10: every sane creature within 60 ft saves (DC 16 Will) or is affected as by *hypnotism* for 2d4 rounds, a sonic mind-affecting compulsion, and a creature that saves is immune to that allip's babble for 24 hours. The cloaker (`NPCDatabase_C`, "Moan") applies Frightened for 2 rounds within 60 ft each turn for free; MM p.36: the moan is a standard action with four selectable effects (unnerve, fear, nausea, stupor), each with its own area and save, cloakers are immune, and a save grants 24-hour immunity to that effect from that cloaker; the fear effect is a 30-ft spread that panics for 2 rounds. The shadow mastiff (`NPCDatabase_S`, "Bay") frightens opponents for 6 rounds; MM p.222: when it howls or barks, all creatures except evil outsiders within a 300-ft spread save (DC 13 Will) or are panicked for 2d4 rounds, a sonic mind-affecting fear effect, and an affected creature is immune to that mastiff's bay for 24 hours whether or not it saves. The mummy (`NPCDatabase_M`, "Despair") frightens opponents within 30 ft for 4 rounds; MM p.190: at the mere sight of the mummy the viewer saves (DC 16 Will) or is paralyzed with fear for 1d4 rounds, and is immune to that mummy's despair for 24 hours whether or not it saves (a sight-triggered effect, like the gazes of CRE-076). The krenshar (`NPCDatabase_K`, "Scare") frightens opponents within 30 ft for 4 rounds every turn for free; MM p.163: the scare is a standard action (a Bluff-like display), and with a screech it works like a *scare* spell from a 3rd-level caster. The opponents-only scope of these auras is the code's default, not RAW (D13; the gibbering and the harpy's captivating song, MM p.126 and p.150, affect allies too since step 19). Read in code and the MM (2026-10-30); not verified in Play mode. Seen in the batch 13 final soak of 2026-10-30 (run 20261030-062539, seed 43, Logs/Scenarios/20261030-062539/soak_dmg-random-encounters__s43.jsonl): the krenshar's Scare fires for no action at the start of each of its 3 turns, before its turn begins (in the midpoint soak it fired inside its AI turn), and frightens 3 of the party for 4 rounds. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_A.cs (`AuraAbility` "Babble"); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_C.cs (`AuraAbility` "Moan"); NPCDatabase_S.cs ("Bay"); NPCDatabase_M.cs ("Despair"); NPCDatabase_K.cs ("Scare"); Assets/Scripts/Combat/Special/GameManager.MonsterTriggers.cs (`ProcessTurnStartAura`). *Fix:* give the babble a 2d4 duration, all sane creatures and the hypnotism effect; make the moan a catalog entry (a standard action with an effect choice for the PC UI and the AI) instead of an aura; give the bay all creatures but evil outsiders, panic for 2d4 rounds and immunity after any save (a new `AuraAbilityDefinition` flag), the despair a sight trigger with paralysis and immunity after any save, and the scare a standard-action catalog entry; batch 16 or 18 with the gaze rules (CRE-076).
- **CRE-080** · **Low** · Player-visible — **A bearer's grapple actions do not fire its frightful presence.** Since batch 13 step 19 (D12) the presence fires on a bearer's attack action, AoO, charge and breath, and on a maneuver that is an attack (`GameManager.ExecuteManeuver`, which starts a grapple), but the attacks a grappling bearer makes on a later turn (`GrappleActionType.DamageOpponent`, `AttackWithLightWeapon`, `AttackUnarmed`, `Rake`) run through the grapple flow (`GrappleSystem`, `CharacterController`), not the attack sequence or the maneuver core, so they never fire it, for a PC or an NPC alike. MM p.309 fires it when the bearer attacks; a grapple attack is an attack. Rare in play (a frightful-presence bearer that grapples, e.g. a dragon with a summon's grab); read in code 2026-10-30, not verified in Play mode. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Combat/Maneuvers/GrappleSystem.cs; Assets/Scripts/Combat/Special/GameManager.MonsterTriggers.cs (`TriggerFrightfulPresence`). *Fix:* call `TriggerFrightfulPresence(bearer, "attacks")` from the grapple attack actions under the same first-attack-of-the-action test (`ProgressiveAttackPool.AttacksCommitted == 0` before the step is paid), with a scenario.

- **CRE-110** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Black Pudding (`black_pudding`) differs from its MM stat block (MM p.201); the ochre jelly (`ochre_jelly`) lacks the same ooze split.** Split (Ex): the game has SpecialAbilities text only and no code splits an ooze, so slashing and piercing weapons damage it normally, the MM has such weapons deal it no damage and split it into two identical puddings with half its current hp each (rounded down), one with 10 hp or less unable to split again and dying at 0 hp. The ochre jelly has the same Split (Ex) (MM p.202 block, p.203 text), triggered by slashing weapons, piercing weapons and electricity: no split logic exists in `CharacterStats.ApplyIncomingDamage` (CharacterStats.cs), `GameManager.DealDamage` (_Core/GameManager.Damage.cs) or the spawn code, so slashing and piercing hits deal it full damage and never split it, and the `DamageImmunities` Electricity entry in `NPCDatabase_O.RegisterOchreJelly` is the only stand-in: it zeroes electricity damage as the MM does but never splits the jelly (the MM block lists split, not an electricity immunity). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs (`Id = "black_pudding"` or its builder); MM p.201; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_O.cs (`RegisterOchreJelly`: Split only in SpecialAbilities, `DamageImmunities = { Electricity }`); MM p.202-203. *Fix:* set these fields to the MM values, then check the spawned totals in the editor and rerun the creature's static suites and the soak; for the split, add an actor-agnostic split rule in the damage path (a per-creature split definition listing its triggers: slashing and piercing for both, plus electricity for the ochre jelly) that zeroes the damage, halves current hp (rounded down) and spawns an identical copy beside it through the encounter spawn and pool reset (CRE-046), with no split at 10 hp or less; then drop the ochre jelly's electricity immunity in favour of that trigger. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.

- **CRE-266** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Natural-attack ability drain is a fixed amount with no dice, no save and no temporary hit points.** `NaturalAttackDefinition` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs) has only `AbilityDrainType` and a fixed `AbilityDrainAmount`. `CharacterController.TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs, ability-drain block) passes that amount straight to `ApplyAbilityDrain` on every hit. There is no drain dice field (only `BloodDrainDefinition` has `AbilityDrainDice`), no Fortitude save DC, and nothing gives the attacker temporary hit points (`CharacterStats.TempHP`) for a successful drain. As a result the allip (`allip`, WIS 1) and the wraith (`wraith`, CON 1) each drain exactly 1 point per hit. RAW: the allip's incorporeal touch deals 1d4 Wisdom drain with no save, and the allip gains 5 temporary hit points on each successful attack (MM p.10). The wraith's incorporeal touch deals 1d4 damage, and a living creature hit must make a DC 14 Fortitude save (Charisma-based) or take 1d6 Constitution drain; the wraith gains 5 temporary hit points on each successful drain (MM p.258). The natural attack also has no ability-damage-on-hit field. So the rolled Strength damage of the shadow (CRE-211, 1d6) and the greater shadow (CRE-125, 1d8) cannot be expressed in data either; both currently use this fixed-drain path. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_A.cs (`Id = "allip"`, `AbilityDrainAmount = 1`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_W.cs (`Id = "wraith"`, `AbilityDrainAmount = 1`); MM p.10, p.258. *Fix:* give `NaturalAttackDefinition` an ability-drain dice count and size, an optional Fortitude DC (rolled with `SaveRollRules.Roll` and `SaveContext.CreatureAbility`), a drain-or-damage choice (`ApplyAbilityDrain` or `ApplyAbilityDamage`) and the temporary hit points gained per successful drain. Copy the new fields in `Clone`, roll the drain in `TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects`, set the allip, wraith, shadow and greater shadow to the MM values, and rerun the creature static suites and the soak. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-267** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Incorporeal creatures get no Charisma deflection bonus to AC.** `CharacterStats.ArmorClass` and `TouchArmorClass` (CharacterStats.cs) take their deflection term only from `EffectiveDeflectionBonus` = max(`DeflectionBonus`, `RingDeflectionBonus`). Only `StatusEffectManager` (spell deflection) and `Inventory.RecalculateStats` (ring deflection) write those fields. `GameManager.InitializeNPCFromDefinition` (GameManager.NPCSetup.cs) passes `NPCDefinition.IsIncorporeal` only to `CharacterController.ConfigureIncorporeal`, which stores the flag for the 50% miss chance, the maneuver bars and `MovementTerrain`. The `NPCDefinition.IsIncorporeal` comment (NPCDatabase.cs) and the shadow's `NaturalArmorBonus` comment promise a deflection bonus from Charisma, but no code derives one. So every incorporeal creature's AC, touch AC and flat-footed AC are short by that bonus. Affected in-scope creatures (as the game would have them, MM values in brackets):
    - `allip`: Cha 18, AC 11 [15, +4 deflection; MM p.10]
    - `shadow`: Cha 13, AC 12 [13, +1; MM p.221]
    - `greater_shadow`: Cha 14, AC 13 [14, +2; MM p.221-222]
    - `spectre`: Cha 15, AC 13 [15, +2; MM p.232, where the printed flat-footed 13 is one more than the arithmetic gives]
    - `wraith`: Cha 15, AC 13 [15, +2; MM p.258]
    - the `ghost` sample: [12, +1 from Cha 12; MM p.117-118]; its Cha and Dex are wrong too (CRE-150).

    Rule: MM p.311 (incorporeal subtype): an incorporeal creature has no natural armor bonus and has a deflection bonus equal to its Charisma bonus, always at least +1. MM p.117 (ghost template): a manifested ghost has natural armor +0 and a deflection bonus equal to its Charisma modifier or +1, whichever is higher. Deflection bonuses do not stack (PHB p.171), so a Shield of Faith or a ring of protection should count only when it beats this bonus. Today CRE-044 (STR/CON 0 makes these creatures spawn dead or Helpless) hides this gap in play. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs (`ArmorClass`, `TouchArmorClass`, `EffectiveDeflectionBonus`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (`ConfigureIncorporeal`); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs (`InitializeNPCFromDefinition`, the `ConfigureIncorporeal(def.IsIncorporeal)` call); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs (`IsIncorporeal` comment); NPCDatabase_A/G/S/W.cs (the six definitions). *Fix:* keep the incorporeal flag on `CharacterStats` (set with `ConfigureIncorporeal` and cleared on slot reuse, CRE-046). Compute an incorporeal deflection of max(1, Cha modifier) at read time, so that Charisma damage or drain changes it. Have `EffectiveDeflectionBonus` take the maximum of that bonus, spell deflection and ring deflection. Then check the six creatures' spawned AC, touch AC and flat-footed AC against the MM, after the CRE-044 fix. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-268** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The derro's madness is text only: its Will saves use Wisdom and it has no confusion or insanity immunity.** `CharacterStats.GetSaveTotal` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs, the default/Will case) always adds `WISMod` (+ `ClassWillSave`, `FeatWillBonus`, the shared bonuses and `ConditionWillModifier`), and no field or rule substitutes another ability on Will saves. `CreatureImmunities` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/CreatureImmunities.cs) has flags for poison, the energy types, disease, mind-affecting, critical hits, sneak attack and weapon damage, but none for confusion or insanity effects, and no condition-immunity check exists in `ConditionService` or elsewhere. So madness lives only in the `SpecialAbilities` string of `NPCDatabase_D.RegisterDerro`. Affected in-scope creature: `derro`. Its WIS 5 / CHA 16 already match the MM (the madness adjustments are built into the listed scores), so the spawned Will is +0 (good base +3, Wis mod -3) instead of the MM's +6 (base +3, Cha mod +3). Lesser confusion, confusion and other confusion or insanity effects still land on it. Rule: MM p.49, madness (Ex): a derro applies its Charisma modifier to Will saves in place of its Wisdom modifier and is immune to confusion and insanity effects. The derro's data errors are CRE-119, which only notes this immunity in passing. *Evidence:* CharacterStats.GetSaveTotal, Will case; CreatureImmunities fields; NPCDatabase_D.cs (`Id = "derro"`, SpecialAbilities "Madness: ..."); grep finds no madness, Cha-for-Will or confusion-immunity rule outside that string; MM p.49. *Fix:* add a Will-save ability override to the creature data (for example a `WillSaveAbility` on `NPCDefinition`, copied in `Clone`/`CopyDefinitionFields` and read by `GetSaveTotal`), and a confusion/insanity immunity (a `CreatureImmunities` flag or effect type checked in `GameManager.ApplyCondition`/`ConditionService` before Confused is applied, and by the AI's target filter). Set both for `derro` and add a static test or scenario for its Will total and for a confusion spell failing on it. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-269** · **Low** — **The flesh golem never goes berserk.** No code implements the golem Berserk (Ex) ability. `GameManager`, `AIService` and `CharacterController` keep no count of a golem's rounds in combat, never roll the cumulative chance and never switch a berserk golem's targeting. The only "berserk" in the code is the `NPCAIProfileArchetype.Berserk` / `BerserkAIProfile` AI tuning profile (Assets/Scripts/AI/Profiles/BerserkAIProfile.cs), which is unrelated. The `flesh_golem` entry (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_F.cs) uses `AIProfileArchetype.Brute` and has the ability only as `SpecialAbilities` display text, whose wording is itself wrong (CRE-167). Affected in-scope creature: `flesh_golem` (the clay golem has the same ability, MM p.134-135, but no definition here). Rule (MM p.135-136): once the flesh golem enters combat it has a cumulative 1% chance each round to go berserk. A berserk golem attacks the nearest living creature, or smashes an object smaller than itself when no creature is in reach. Its creator, if within 60 ft., can try to regain control with a DC 19 Charisma check. One minute (10 rounds) without activity resets the chance to 0%. Today the golem spawns alone ("1x Flesh Golem" in Assets/Scripts/Encounters/DungeonEncounterTableData.cs) with no creator, so the visible difference is small: the berserk golem's choice of target (always the nearest living creature, its own side included) instead of the Brute profile's choice. *Evidence:* a grep of Assets/Scripts for golem and berserk finds no rule code (only `CharacterStats.IsGolem`, used for vorpal beheading, an AI profile comment and the encounter tables); NPCDatabase_F.cs (`Id = "flesh_golem"`); MM p.135-136. *Fix:* add per-creature berserk state, a chance and a berserk flag, cleared in `ResetForNewCreature` (CRE-046). Raise the chance by 1% at the start of each of the golem's rounds in combat and roll it. Once berserk, make the golem target the nearest living creature on either side (and objects, if the game ever has them). Let a creator within 60 ft. attempt the DC 19 Charisma check. Reset the chance after 10 rounds of inactivity. Fix the CRE-167 text at the same time. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-270** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Natural-attack paralysis lasts a fixed number of rounds: no duration dice can be stored.** On a failed Fort save, `CharacterController.TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs, the paralysis-on-hit block) applies Paralyzed for `Mathf.Max(1, naturalAttack.ParalysisOnHitDurationRounds)` rounds. That is a fixed int. `NaturalAttackDefinition` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs) has only `ParalysisOnHitDC` and `ParalysisOnHitDurationRounds`, with no dice count, die size or bonus for the duration. `EngulfDefinition.ParalysisDurationRounds` (NPCDatabase.cs) is written but never read. So every paralysis always lasts exactly the stored number of rounds. Affected in-scope creatures: `gelatinous_cube` (Slam, fixed 4 rounds; MM p.202 gives 3d6 rounds, also on its engulf), `ghoul` (Bite and Claw, fixed 4; MM p.118 gives 1d4+1 rounds) and `ghast` (Bite and Claw, fixed 4; MM p.119 gives 1d4+1 rounds). The same cause also blocks the mohrg tongue (1d4 minutes, CRE-207) and the carrion crawler tentacles (2d4 rounds, CRE-171). The Ghoul Touch spell rolls its own duration (`GhoulTouchEffectData.RollParalysisDuration`) and is not affected. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:6148; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs (`gelatinous_cube` Slam and Engulf, `ghast` and `ghoul` NaturalAttacks); MM p.118-119, p.202. *Fix:* add duration dice fields to `NaturalAttackDefinition` (count, sides, bonus, and a unit multiplier for minutes), roll them with `DiceRoller.Roll` in the on-hit block and keep the fixed int as a fallback. Set the cube to 3d6, the ghoul and ghast to 1d4+1, the mohrg to 1d4 x 10 rounds and the carrion crawler to 2d4. Clear the new fields in `UndeadTemplateUtils.StripSpecialEffects`. Then read or remove `EngulfDefinition.ParalysisDurationRounds` when engulf paralysis is built (CRE-148). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-271** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Golem immunity to magic is text only: spells that allow SR affect the flesh golem, and cold, fire and electricity do not slow or heal it.** The flesh golem's immunity to magic exists only as a SpecialAbilities string (`NPCDatabase_F.RegisterFleshGolem`, Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_F.cs; the string also swaps fire and electricity, CRE-167). `CreatureImmunities` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/CreatureImmunities.cs) and `NPCDefinition` have no field for immunity to magic or for an energy type that slows or heals. No spell path checks for it either: `SpellCaster.Cast`, the cast core (`GameManager.ExecuteCast`, GameManager.CastCore.cs) and the area handlers stop a spell only through SR, and `SpellCastingHelper.IsBlockedBySpellResistance` and the `SpellSaveResolver` SR checks treat a target with `SpellResistance` 0 as unprotected. So every SR-allowing spell or spell-like ability lands on the golem in full. Magical cold and fire do ordinary damage and never slow it, and magical electricity damages it instead of breaking the slow and healing it. Affected in-scope creature: `flesh_golem`. The will-o'-wisp's immunity to magic (`will_o_wisp`, filed as data in CRE-192) is the same missing mechanism. Rule: MM p.136 makes the flesh golem immune to any spell or spell-like ability that allows SR. A magical attack dealing cold or fire slows it as the slow spell for 2d6 rounds with no save. A magical attack dealing electricity ends any slow and heals 1 hp per 3 points of damage it would deal, with healing above full normal hp kept as temporary hp, and the golem gets no save against it. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_F.cs (`Id = "flesh_golem"`, Immunities and SpecialAbilities); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/CreatureImmunities.cs; Assets/Scripts/Spell/Casting/SpellCastingHelper.cs (`IsBlockedBySpellResistance` returns false when `SpellResistance <= 0`); grepping Assets/Scripts finds no immunity-to-magic or golem rule in the spell or damage code (the only golem mention is a comment in Spell/Casting/AreaDamageRoll.cs); MM p.136. *Fix:* add an actor-agnostic immunity-to-magic rule (a `CreatureImmunities` flag plus a per-creature list of exceptions and energy reactions).
