Have the cast core, `SpellCaster.Cast` and the area loop skip a target that has the flag for any SR-allowing spell or spell-like ability. Route magical cold and fire through `GameManager.DealDamage` into a no-save 2d6-round slow, and magical electricity into healing at 1 per 3 points with the excess as temporary hp plus removal of the slow. Share the flag with the will-o'-wisp (CRE-192) and cover it in a rules scenario. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-272** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Disease incubation is always counted in days, so mummy rot's 1-minute incubation cannot be expressed.** `DiseaseData.IncubationPeriod` is a dice string. `DiseaseData.RollIncubationDays` rolls it and clamps it to at least 1 (Assets/Scripts/Effects/Disease.cs). `ActiveDisease` stores the result as `DaysUntilActive`, and only `CharacterController.ProcessDiseaseEffectsDaily` counts it down, one step per day. The model has no minute or round unit, and the contraction log (`CharacterController.ExposeToDisease`) and `StatusEffectIndicator` both show the value as days. `DiseaseDatabase` gives MummyRot an IncubationPeriod of "1" (the DMG disease-table value), so a victim of the mummy (`mummy`) incubates mummy rot for 1 day. MM p.190 gives mummy rot an incubation of 1 minute (10 rounds), so it should take hold within the same fight. The effect stays latent while daily disease progression never runs (CORE-035); the mummy rot save DC and damage are CRE-244. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Effects/Disease.cs (`RollIncubationDays`, `ActiveDisease` constructor); Assets/Scripts/Effects/DiseaseDatabase.cs (MummyRot entry); Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (`ExposeToDisease` log, `ProcessDiseaseEffectsDaily`); Assets/Scripts/UI/Combat/StatusEffectIndicator.cs; MM p.190. *Fix:* give `DiseaseData` an incubation unit (rounds, minutes or days). Count round- and minute-based incubations down on the combat turn clock (TurnDurations) and day-based ones on the daily clock. Set MummyRot to 1 minute, make the log and status text show the unit, and add a static test. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-273** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The stirge's blood drain is 1 point of Con drain per touch hit instead of 1d4 Con damage each round it begins attached.** `CharacterController.TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs) runs whenever a natural attack with `HasBloodDrain` hits. It calls `target.ApplyAbilityDrain(AbilityType.CON, BloodDrainConDamagePerRound)` with the stirge's flat value of 1, so every touch hit deals permanent Con drain. The MM gives Con damage, which heals normally. The rider runs only from the attack paths. Attach is modelled as Improved Grab, but the grapple hold and its grapple-check damage (the touch deals 0) never drain. So an attached stirge deals nothing at the start of its turn, keeps no running total, and never detaches after 4 points or when its victim dies. Affects `stirge`. MM p.237 (stirge, Blood Drain and Attach): the stirge deals 1d4 points of Con damage in any round it begins its turn attached to a victim. Once it has dealt 4 points in total it detaches and flies off. If the victim dies first, it detaches and seeks a new target. CRE-031 covers only the unread grapple `BloodDrainDefinition` and calls this path the working one. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:6189-6194 (the rider; callers at 6752 and 6951); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_S.cs:103 (`HasBloodDrain = true, BloodDrainConDamagePerRound = 1`); docs/systems/CREATURES.md Blood drain row; MM p.236-237. *Fix:* drop the on-hit drain. At the start of the stirge's turn, while it holds its grabbed victim, roll 1d4 and deal it as Con ability damage through `ApplyAbilityDamage`, keeping a per-attachment total (cleared in `ResetForNewCreature`). Release the hold once the total reaches 4 or the victim dies. Then add a rules scenario. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-274** · **Low** · Player-visible — **No creature can be immune to petrification.** `CreatureImmunities` (Character/Creatures/CreatureImmunities.cs) has no petrification field, and `CreatureEffectImmunityType` offers only Poison, Disease, MindAffecting, CriticalHits and SneakAttack. As a result, none of the paths that apply `CombatConditionType.Petrified` can refuse it. The on-hit petrification in `CharacterController` (the `PetrificationOnHitDC` block: Fortitude save, then `Conditions.ApplyCondition(Petrified)`) and the gaze aura mapping in `AIService` (`AuraEffectType.Petrified`, the basilisk and medusa gazes) check no immunity. The gorgon breath and Flesh to Stone have no flag to check either. The in-scope creatures whose MM entries give immunity to petrification have it only as SpecialAbilities text: `formian_taskmaster` and `formian_worker` (MM p.109-110), `lantern_archon` and `hound_archon` (archon traits, MM p.16-17) and `bralani` (eladrin traits, MM p.93-94). A summoned lantern archon (`SummonMonsterLists`) can therefore be turned to stone by a cockatrice, basilisk or medusa. *Evidence:* CreatureImmunities.cs (fields, `IsImmuneToEffect`, `MergeFrom`, `Clone`, `GetDisplayTraits`); CharacterController.cs (petrification on hit); AIService.cs (aura effect mapping); NPCDatabase_F.cs, NPCDatabase_L.cs, NPCDatabase_H.cs and NPCDatabase_B.cs (text-only immunity lines). *Fix:* add an `immuneToPetrification` flag, a `Petrification` value in `CreatureEffectImmunityType`, and matching lines in `MergeFrom`, `Clone` and `GetDisplayTraits`. Check the flag in every path that applies Petrified (on hit, gaze aura, breath weapon, Flesh to Stone), preferably at one shared gate. Set it on the five definitions above, then add a rules scenario (a cockatrice biting a lantern archon). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-275** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Giant rock throwing is text only: giants have no ranged rock attack.** The giant definitions carry a "RockThrowing" `CreatureTags` entry and a "Rock Throwing (Ex)" `SpecialAbilities` string, but no code reads either one. No rock item exists, and the attack code (`GameManager.RunAttackSequence`, `CharacterController.BuildAttackBonus`), the action catalog (`Combat/Actions`) and the AI have no rock-throw attack or action. So a giant fights only with its melee weapon and never attacks at range. Affected in-scope creatures: `stone_giant` (NPCDatabase_S.cs, `RegisterStoneGiant`; empty `NaturalAttacks`, greatclub and hide armor only) and `hill_giant` (NPCDatabase_H.cs). `frost_giant` and `fire_giant` (NPCDatabase_F.cs) have the same tag and the same gap. Rule: adult giants throw rocks as a ranged attack with a +1 racial bonus on the attack roll and a reach of up to five range increments; a Large giant hurls Small rocks and a Huge giant Medium ones (MM p.119-120). The stone giant's rock is +11 ranged for 2d8+12, range increment 180 ft., thrown with both hands, and can be used as its single attack or full attack (MM p.124). The hill giant's rock is +8 ranged for 2d6+7 (MM p.123), range increment 120 ft. (MM p.124). The hill giant's printed bonus includes the +1 racial bonus (BAB 9, Dex -1, size -1, +1). The stone giant's printed +11 does not (BAB 10, Dex +2, size -1 gives +11, or +12 with the racial bonus), so the fix should settle this with a delegated RAW reading in RULES_COVERAGE 4.1. Rock catching is not modelled either (CRE-225 for the stone giant's text). *Evidence:* the only hits for RockThrowing and "Rock Throwing" in Assets/Scripts are the definitions at NPCDatabase_S.cs:578/580, NPCDatabase_H.cs:446/448 and NPCDatabase_F.cs:294/296 and 336/338; docs/systems/CREATURES.md ("Text only") and docs/systems/AI.md (option matrix) list it as text-only backlog; MM p.119-120, p.123, p.124. *Fix:* add a rock-throw ranged attack that is actor-agnostic and goes through the shared attack sequence. Its attack modifier comes from `BuildAttackBonus` with the +1 racial bonus, and its damage and Strength terms come from `BuildWeaponDamageBonus` (the stone giant throws two-handed). It needs range increments up to five, and it must count as a standard attack and as a full-attack option. Register it in the action catalog, give the AI a use for it (tactics inventory `ability_rock_throwing`, docs/systems/tactics/CREATURES_L5-8.md), drive it from the RockThrowing tag plus per-creature damage and range increment, and add scenario coverage. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-276** · **Low** · Player-visible — **A disease's save DC is one shared value, so creatures that spread the same disease cannot have their own DC.** `CharacterController.TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` (`Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs`) exposes the target with `ExposeToDisease(naturalAttack.DiseaseOnHitType, dcModifier)`, and the only modifier it passes is `WildShapeRules.OnHitSaveDcModifier`, which is 0 for every creature in its own form. `ExposeToDisease` then rolls the Fortitude save against the single `FortitudeDC` of that disease type in `DiseaseDatabase` (`Effects/DiseaseDatabase.cs`). `NaturalAttackDefinition` has only `HasDiseaseOnHit` and `DiseaseOnHitType` and no DC field. In the MM, though, the DC belongs to each creature's ability and is figured from its own HD and Charisma or Constitution. Affected in-scope creatures: `ghoul` and `ghast` both get ghoul fever at the shared DC 14, but the ghoul's is Fort DC 12 (MM p.118) and the ghast's Fort DC 15 (MM p.119), both Charisma-based; `dire_rat` and `fiendish_dire_rat` get filth fever at the shared DC 12, but the dire rat's is Fort DC 11 (Constitution-based, MM p.64), while the otyugh's is DC 12 (MM p.205). Changing the shared value cannot make both creatures of either pair right. Disease progression never runs in play at present (CORE-035), so for now the wrong DC changes only whether an infection is recorded and the DC shown in the combat log. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs (`TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects`, `ExposeToDisease`); Assets/Scripts/Effects/DiseaseDatabase.cs (GhoulFever, FilthFever); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_G.cs (`ghast`, `ghoul` bites), NPCDatabase_D.cs (`dire_rat`), NPCDatabase_F.cs (`fiendish_dire_rat`); MM p.64, p.118, p.119, p.205. *Fix:* give `NaturalAttackDefinition` a per-attack disease save DC (copied in `NPCDefinition.Clone`, the templates' `CopyDefinitionFields` and the CharacterStats copy). Have the on-hit rider pass it to `ExposeToDisease` in place of the database DC, with the wild-shape modifier still applied on top, and keep the `DiseaseDatabase` DC only as the fallback for exposures without a creature source. Set ghoul 12, ghast 15 and dire rat 11 (fiendish dire rat 11). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-277** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Burn (catch fire on a hit) is not modelled for any creature.** `NaturalAttackDefinition` (`Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs`) has on-hit riders for paralysis, energy drain, ability drain, disease, petrification, poison, energy dice and blood drain, but none that sets a creature on fire after a failed Reflex save. No catch-fire or burning condition exists anywhere in `Assets/Scripts`: the only burning state is the Web spell's `WebAreaEffect._isBurning`. So a creature hit by a slam with Burn never catches fire, never takes the burning damage and never has to spend an action putting the flame out. Affected in-scope creatures: `thoqqua` (MM p.242: on a slam hit, DC 12 Reflex (Con-based) or catch fire for 1d4 rounds unless put out; putting it out takes a full-round action; MM p.242 refers to DMG p.303 Catching on Fire). The thoqqua's `SpecialAbilities` text in `NPCDatabase_T.RegisterThoqqua` does not even name Burn. Also affected: `small_fire_elemental` (DC 11) and `medium_fire_elemental` (DC 14). MM p.98: their slam sets the target on fire for 1d4 rounds on a failed Reflex save, a move action puts the flame out, and a creature that hits the elemental with a natural weapon or an unarmed attack takes its fire damage and must save the same way. Their Burn is only a `SpecialAbilities` text line in `NPCDatabase_E.cs`, as docs/systems/CREATURES.md notes. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs (`NaturalAttackDefinition`, no ignite field); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_T.cs (`RegisterThoqqua`, Slam with no rider); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_E.cs (small and medium fire elementals, Burn as text only). *Fix:* add a catch-fire rider to `NaturalAttackDefinition`: a Reflex DC (Con-based), a duration in dice and the action that puts the flame out. Roll the save with `SaveRollRules.Roll` in the shared natural-attack on-hit block. Add a burning condition that deals its fire damage through `GameManager.DealDamage` each round (DMG p.303) and ends early when the creature spends the stated action. Give the fire elementals the reverse trigger for creatures that hit them with natural weapons or unarmed attacks. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-278** · **Med** · Player-visible — **A creature's racial deflection bonus to AC cannot be expressed, so the will-o'-wisp loses 9 AC.** `NPCDefinition` (`Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs`) has no deflection or other racial AC field. `CharacterStats.ArmorClass` and `TouchArmorClass` add deflection only through `EffectiveDeflectionBonus`, the higher of `DeflectionBonus` and `RingDeflectionBonus`. `DeflectionBonus` is written only by `StatusEffectManager.RecomputeSpellDeflection` (the best applied spell; setup leaves it alone except the test-only `GameManager.ApplyDisruptUndeadTestEasyHitOverrides`), and `RingDeflectionBonus` only by `Inventory.RecalculateStats`. So a creature whose stat block includes an innate deflection bonus has none in play. Affected in-scope creature: `will_o_wisp`. MM p.255 gives it AC 29, touch 29, flat-footed 20 from +1 size, +9 Dex and +9 deflection; the game gives 20, touch 20, flat-footed 11. Its touch-attack defence is the most affected. *Evidence:* `NPCDatabase_W.RegisterWillOWisp` (DEX 29, Small, `NaturalArmorBonus` 0, no deflection data); AC formula at `CharacterStats.cs` about line 4320-4345; no writer of a racial deflection anywhere under `Assets/Scripts`. *Fix:* add a racial deflection field to `NPCDefinition` (copied in `Clone` and the templates' `CopyDefinitionFields`, CRE-023). Copy it in `GameManager.NPCSetup` into a separate `CharacterStats` field, cleared in `ResetForNewCreature`, so the spell recompute cannot overwrite it. Fold it into `EffectiveDeflectionBonus` as a third source, highest one applies (same-type bonuses do not stack, PHB p.171), and set 9 for the will-o'-wisp. The same source could carry the incorporeal Charisma deflection (MM p.311). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-279** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Mephit fast healing works everywhere instead of only in the mephit's element.** `NPCDatabase.MephitBase` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_M.cs) sets `RegenerationAmount = 2` for every mephit, with no condition. `CharacterController.ApplyRegenerationAtTurnStart` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs, called from `GameManager.TurnStart.cs`) heals that amount at every turn start. Its only gate is the damage-tag suppression set in `NotifyIncomingDamage`, and the game has no environment model (terrain wetness, rain, aridity, heat and humidity, temperature, or touching fire or ice), so the environment wording in each `fastHealingDesc` is text only. The MM says mephits heal only when their own entry's condition is met (MM p.181). Affected: `ooze_mephit` (wet or muddy surroundings, MM p.184), `salt_mephit` (arid surroundings, MM p.184), `steam_mephit` (touching boiling water, or in a hot, humid area, MM p.184), `water_mephit` (in rain, or in water up to its waist, MM p.185). The same code affects `dust_mephit` (arid and dusty, MM p.181), `earth_mephit` (underground or buried to the waist, MM p.182), `fire_mephit` (touching a flame at least torch-sized, MM p.182), `ice_mephit` (touching ice of at least Tiny size, or below-freezing air, MM p.183) and `magma_mephit` (touching magma, lava or a torch-sized flame, MM p.183). `air_mephit` is close to RAW: any moving air counts, its own fanning included (MM p.181). So on an ordinary battlefield, nine of the ten mephits heal 2 hp a round when they should not. CRE-017 covers only fast healing being stored as regeneration, not this gate. *Evidence:* NPCDatabase_M.cs `MephitBase` (`RegenerationAmount = 2`) and the `Register*Mephit` `fastHealingDesc` strings; CharacterController.cs `ApplyRegenerationAtTurnStart` (about line 5227) and `NotifyIncomingDamage`; no environment or terrain-condition type exists under Assets/Scripts. *Fix:* give `NPCDefinition` a fast-healing condition (an enum per mephit element) and check it in the turn-start heal against a battlefield environment or terrain model, once one exists. Until then, either treat the condition as unmet in a normal encounter, unless the encounter declares the element, or let a mephit heal only when the condition is met by something the game does model (a fire effect next to a fire or magma mephit, its own movement for the air mephit). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-280** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Rend is not implemented: a creature that hits one foe with both claws in a full attack deals no extra rend damage.** Rend exists only as display text: a `SpecialAbilities` string and a comment or `Description` in the creature's database entry. No `NPCDefinition` or `CharacterStats` field exists for it, `GameManager.RunAttackSequence` (`_Core/GameManager.AttackCore.cs`) does not count claw hits on a target, and none of the `GameManager.OnAttackResolved` stages (`_Core/GameManager.AttackResolved.cs`: thrown/ammo, concentration, melee reactions, attacked triggers, free trip, Improved Grab, Cleave) adds the damage. So a creature that should rend deals only its claw and bite damage, and the AI cannot weigh rend when it picks a full attack. Affected in-scope creatures: `troll` (MM p.247: an extra 2d6+9 when both claws hit; text at `NPCDatabase_T.cs:97`), `annis` (MM p.143: 2d6+10 when both claws hit; `NPCDatabase_A.cs:224`), `dire_ape` (MM p.62: 2d6+9 when both claws hit; `NPCDatabase_D.cs:902`; summoned by Summon Nature's Ally IV), and `girallon` (MM p.126-127: one extra 2d4+9 when two or more of its four claws hit; `NPCDatabase_G.cs:1250`, whose text also says 2d4+12, wrong against the MM). Rule: MM p.314 (rend glossary) says a creature that hits with the listed natural attacks latches on and tears the flesh for an automatic extra amount, about the attack's damage plus 1-1/2 times its Str modifier, with the exact figure in the creature's entry. *Evidence:* a search of `Assets/Scripts` outside the `NPCDatabase_*.cs` files finds no rend code. `docs/systems/tactics/CREATURES_L5-8.md` and `SUMMONS.md` list `ability_rend` as missing for these creatures. *Fix:* add rend data (the triggering attack name, the hits needed, the damage dice and bonus) to `NPCDefinition`, including `Clone` and the templates' `CopyDefinitionFields`, and copy it to the stats. In the shared attack sequence, track qualifying hits per target and, once the threshold is reached, deal the rend damage once per full attack through `GameManager.DealDamage(..., DamagePackets.CreatureAttack(...))`, so DR and the other mitigations apply, then run `EvaluateCombatEnd`. Clear the per-sequence state in `ResetForNewCreature`. Add the expected rend damage to the AI's full-attack estimate. Correct the girallon text to 2d4+9. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-281** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The weretiger's hybrid form has pounce, a tiger-form ability.** The game registers lycanthropes in hybrid form only (CRE-011). Even so, `LycanthropeTemplate.Apply` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs) sets `lycan.HasPounce = true` whenever the animal is a tiger, and its comment wrongly says the MM lets the weretiger hybrid pounce. As a result, `weretiger` ("Weretiger (Hybrid)", built by `LycanthropeFactory.Weretiger`) makes a full attack at the end of every charge with its 2 claws and bite. The flag reaches play through `GameManager.NPCSetup` (`stats.HasPounce = def.HasPounce`) and through the lycanthrope template adapter (`LycanthropeTemplateBase`, `target.HasPounce = source.HasPounce`). It also drives the `AnimalAIProfile` pounce choice, the special-abilities text ("Pounce ... in hybrid form") and the factory's description. Improved grab and rake are already cleared correctly. RAW: in the MM weretiger stat block, curse of lycanthropy is the hybrid form's only special attack, and pounce, improved grab and rake belong to the tiger form (MM p.174). The text says the weretiger pounces and grabs as a tiger and fights with its claws in hybrid form (MM p.172). The template gives the hybrid none of the base animal's special attacks (MM p.176). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs (`Apply`, the Tiger branch of the special-attack block; the abilities text; `LycanthropeFactory.Weretiger`); Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.NPCSetup.cs (`HasPounce`). *Fix:* set `HasPounce = false` for every hybrid form (drop the Tiger branch) and correct the comment, the abilities text and the description; give pounce back to the tiger form when form shifting is built (CRE-011). Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-282** · **Low** — **Metallic dragons' secondary breath copies the primary breath's shape and range.** `NPCDatabase.BuildSecondaryBreathWeapon` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_Dragons.cs, `Shape = template.BreathShape`, `RangeFeet = stats.BreathRangeFeet`, with a comment saying the gas uses the primary's shape and range) builds every gas with the primary breath's shape and length. For the metallic dragons whose primary breath is a line (brass, copper and bronze templates in DragonData.cs, `BreathShape = Line`), the gas therefore becomes a line as long as the primary, not a cone of the dragon's size. Gold and silver are unaffected because their primary breath is already a cone. In scope: `dragon_brass_wyrmling` gets a 30-ft sleep-gas line where the MM gives a 15-ft cone (Tiny), and `dragon_copper_young` gets a 40-ft slow-gas line where the MM gives a 30-ft cone (Medium; its primary line is also wrong, 40 ft instead of 60 ft, CRE-262). The bronze repulsion gas has the same defect (e.g. `dragon_bronze_juvenile`: an 80-ft line instead of a 40-ft cone). Rule: MM p.69 (breath area by dragon size: the cone is half the line's length, Tiny 15 ft, Medium 30 ft, Large 40 ft); MM p.80 (brass: fire line or sleep-gas cone); MM p.83 (copper: acid line or slow-gas cone); MM p.81-82 (bronze: lightning line or repulsion-gas cone). The defect is latent: AI-008 gives the gas no execution path, and the special-ability text never shows its shape or range. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_Dragons.cs (`BuildSecondaryBreathWeapon`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs (the Brass, Copper and Bronze templates, `BreathShape = BreathWeaponShape.Line`); MM p.69, p.80, p.82-83. *Fix:* always build the gas as a cone (`BreathWeaponShape.Cone`) whose length is the MM p.69 cone length for the dragon's size (half the line length), derived from `size` rather than `stats.BreathRangeFeet`; fix it together with AI-008's executor. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-283** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The zombie template slows every zombie by 10 ft.** `ZombieTemplate.Apply` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/ZombieTemplate.cs:118-120, commented "reduce by 10 ft") sets `zombie.BaseSpeed = Mathf.Max(4, baseCreature.BaseSpeed - 2)`. The MM zombie template leaves land speed as it is. Its Speed entry only drops a flier's maneuverability to clumsy (MM p.265), and every sample keeps the base creature's speed and adds only "can't run": human commoner zombie 30 ft (MM p.266), minotaur zombie 30 ft and ogre zombie 40 ft (MM p.267). As a result `zombie_human_commoner` (inline base BaseSpeed 6) and `zombie_minotaur` (`BaseCreatureDefinitions.Minotaur`, BaseSpeed 6) move 4 squares (20 ft) instead of 6 (30 ft). The same formula also slows the other `ZombieFactory` entries, for example the ogre zombie to 30 ft against the MM's 40 ft, and the troglodyte and bugbear zombies to 20 ft against 30 ft. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/ZombieTemplate.cs:120 (`ZombieTemplate.Apply`), `ZombieFactory.HumanCommonerZombie` and `ZombieFactory.MinotaurZombie` (nothing resets the speed after Apply); Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/BaseCreatureDefinitions.cs:74 (minotaur BaseSpeed 6); MM p.265-267. *Fix:* keep `zombie.BaseSpeed = baseCreature.BaseSpeed` (and the other movement modes), keep the clumsy-flight note, fix the comment, and add a static assertion that a template zombie keeps its base land speed. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-284** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Lesser dragons are one size too small at young and young adult age.** `DragonData.GetSize` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs) maps the Lesser size class (white, black, brass, copper) for ages 1-6 to Tiny, Small, Small, Medium, Medium, Large, and the comment above it repeats that sequence. Every Lesser age table in the MM instead gives Tiny, Small, Medium, Medium, Large, Large (white MM p.77, black MM p.70, brass MM p.79, copper MM p.83). A young Lesser dragon is therefore built Small where the MM makes it Medium, and a young adult Medium where the MM makes it Large. `NPCDatabase_Dragons.BuildDragonDefinition` takes the definition's size from this method and passes it to `BuildDragonNaturalAttacks` and the secondary breath, so the size modifiers to AC, attack and grapple, the space and reach and the natural-attack damage dice are all one step off. In-scope creatures affected: `dragon_white_young` (MM p.77-78: Medium) and `dragon_copper_young` (MM p.82-83: Medium); `dragon_brass_wyrmling` is correctly Tiny. CRE-262 and CRE-263 cover these two dragons' other fields and do not mention size. Out of scope but in the same method: the Ordinary branch matches the MM, but the Greater branch (Small, Medium, Large, Large, Huge, Huge) matches no MM table. Red (MM p.75) and gold (MM p.84) run Medium, Large, Large, Large, Huge, Huge, and silver (MM p.87) follows the Ordinary sequence. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs (`GetSize`, Lesser and Greater branches and their comment); Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_Dragons.cs (`BuildDragonDefinition`); MM p.70, 75, 77, 79, 83, 84, 87 age tables. *Fix:* make the Lesser branch return Tiny, Small, Medium, Medium, Large, Large. Fix the Greater branch, or keep each type's size per age in its template (red and gold Medium, Large, Large, Large, Huge, Huge; silver Small, Medium, Medium, Large, Large, Huge). Correct the comment, then check the spawned AC, attack, reach and damage of the affected dragons. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-285** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The lycanthrope template always names the hybrid's secondary attack Bite, so the wereboar gores with a bite.** `LycanthropeTemplate.Apply` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs) builds the hybrid's secondary natural attack with the fixed name "Bite" for every base animal. It sizes the dice with `GetHybridBiteDamageDice`, gives it half Str, sets `IsPrimary = false` and puts the curse (`HasDiseaseOnHit`, `DiseaseType.Lycanthropy`) on it. `LycanthropeFactory.Wereboar` keeps that name. The dice, the secondary flag and the half-Str damage match the MM; only the name is wrong. Because `CharacterStats.GetDefaultPhysicalDamageTypes` reads the attack's name, the attack also deals bludgeoning, piercing and slashing damage instead of piercing only. It therefore gets past DR/bludgeoning and DR/slashing, and checks that go by name, such as sunder, treat it as a bite. Affected in-scope creature: `wereboar`. The dire wereboar (`dire_wereboar`) is correct with a bite, because the MM p.175 sidebar gives it one. Rule: the general template text on MM p.175 gives a hybrid two claws and a bite. The wereboar's own entry, MM p.171-172, gives its hybrid a secondary gore (1d6+1) next to the battleaxe or the two claws and spreads the curse through the gore. The creature's own entry overrides the general template. *Evidence:* LycanthropeTemplate.cs `Apply` (`Name = "Bite"` on the secondary natural attack) and `LycanthropeFactory.Wereboar`; CharacterStats.cs `GetDefaultPhysicalDamageTypes` (bite gives B/P/S, gore gives P); MM p.171-172, 175. *Fix:* give the base animal data a hybrid secondary attack name (Gore for `LycanthropeAnimalType.Boar` only, Bite for the rest, the dire boar included) and use it in `Apply`, keeping the curse on that attack. Then rerun the lycanthrope static suites and the soak. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-286** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Lycanthrope CR truncates a fractional total instead of rounding up.** `LycanthropeTemplate.CalculateLycanthropeCR` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs, called from `LycanthropeTemplate.Apply`) adds the animal-HD modifier to the parsed base CR and returns `((int)totalCR)`. A base CR of 1/2 (a 1st-level NPC-class human, the base of every MM sample lycanthrope) therefore loses the half. The `werebear` (base "1/2" in `LycanthropeFactory.Werebear`, brown bear 6 HD, +4) spawns at CR 4, and its XP and encounter level are computed from that. MM p.177 says a lycanthrope's CR is the base creature's CR plus a modifier from the base animal's HD (1-2 HD +2, 3-5 HD +3, 6-10 HD +4, 11-20 HD +5, 21+ HD +6) and gives no rounding rule. MM p.171 builds the werebear on a 1st-level human warrior (CR 1/2) and lists it at CR 5. The other MM samples on the same base also round 1/2 + modifier up: werewolf 3, wereboar 4, weretiger 5 (MM p.171-175). The wererat (MM p.173, CR 2) is the one exception. The code reaches the MM werewolf and wereboar values only because their bases claim CR "1" instead of 1/2; the weretiger's CR is filed as data in CRE-250. *Evidence:* LycanthropeTemplate.cs `CalculateLycanthropeCR` (`return ((int)totalCR).ToString();`) and `LycanthropeFactory.Werebear` (`ChallengeRating = "1/2"`, `LycanthropeAnimalType.BrownBear`, AnimalHD 6); MM p.171, p.177. *Fix:* round a fractional total up (the reading that four of the five MM samples follow; record it as a delegated RAW reading in `docs/systems/RULES_COVERAGE.md` 4.1). Give the 1st-level-warrior bases CR 1/2, and keep the wererat at its printed CR 2 with an explicit override like the dire wereboar's. Alternatively, set the werebear's CR to 5 explicitly. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-287** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Dragons are not immune to sleep and paralysis.** `NPCDatabase.BuildDragonDefinition` (`Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_Dragons.cs`) sets only the colour's energy immunity (`CreatureImmunities.immuneToFire`/`Cold`/`Acid`/`Electricity` plus `DamageImmunities`). Its comment says sleep and paralysis are "handled via condition immunity in creature type", but no such code exists. `CreatureImmunities` (`Character/Creatures/CreatureImmunities.cs`) has no sleep or paralysis field, and no creature-type trait code exists. `SpellUtilities.IsImmuneToSleepEffects` (`Spell/Casting/SpellUtilities.cs`) tests only `IsImmuneToMindAffecting` and `Race.ImmunityToSleep`, and it is the only gate for Sleep and Deep Slumber (`GameManager.SpellCasting.cs`). `ConditionService.ApplyCondition` and `ConditionManager.ApplyCondition` block only fear (`BlocksFearCondition`), so Hold Monster (`ApplyHoldMonsterEffect`, `GameManager_Spells_Phase1.cs`), Ghoul Touch (`CharacterController.ApplyGhoulTouchEffect`) and on-hit paralysis (`ParalysisOnHitDC`, `CharacterController.cs`) all paralyze a dragon. The immunity appears only as description and SpecialAbilities text. This affects every dragon from the builder. The in-scope ones found are `dragon_brass_wyrmling` (4 HD, so even Sleep's 4 HD cap lets it through), `dragon_copper_young` and `dragon_white_young`. Rule: every dragon is immune to magic sleep and paralysis effects (MM p.69; dragon type, MM p.308; sample blocks MM p.78, p.80, p.83). *Evidence:* the `// --- Immunities ---` block of `BuildDragonDefinition`; `SpellUtilities.IsImmuneToSleepEffects`; `ConditionManager.ApplyCondition`. *Fix:* add sleep and paralysis immunity to `CreatureImmunities` (with `MergeFrom`, `Clone` and the display traits), set it for the Dragon type (and the other types that grant it), read it in `IsImmuneToSleepEffects` and in one condition-entry check, `ConditionManager.ApplyCondition` for Paralyzed from a paralysis effect, so that spells and on-hit attacks share it. Add a static or rules test. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-288** · **Med** · Player-visible — **The zombie template makes the added slam a secondary attack whenever the base creature has another natural attack.** `ZombieTemplate.Apply` (Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/ZombieTemplate.cs, the slam block after the existing slams are removed) sets `hasOtherNaturalAttacks = zombie.NaturalAttacks.Count > 0`. It then adds the slam with `IsPrimary = !hasOtherNaturalAttacks` and `DamageBonusSource.StrengthHalf`, so `CharacterStats.GetNaturalAttackSequencePenalty` gives it -5 (zombies have Toughness, not Multiattack) and it adds only half Strength to damage. Affected in-scope creatures: zombie_minotaur (it keeps the minotaur's primary gore, so the slam is +5 for 1d8+2 against the MM's +10 for 1d8+5, MM p.267) and zombie_troglodyte (it keeps claws and bite, so the slam is -2 for 1d6+0 against the MM's +3 for 1d6+1, MM p.266). The owlbear zombie, which has no MM stat block, gets a secondary slam the same way. RAW: the template only says a zombie gains a slam (MM p.266). Every MM zombie stat block lists the slam at the full attack bonus as one of its single-attack alternatives. With no other natural attack, the slam gets 1.5 times Strength (bugbear zombie 1d6+4 at Str 17, ogre zombie 1d8+9 at Str 23, MM p.267). Next to another natural attack, it gets full Strength (minotaur zombie 1d8+5 at Str 21). This is the MM's specific reading of the general primary/secondary rule (MM p.312). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/ZombieTemplate.cs (`ZombieTemplate.Apply`, `ZombieFactory.MinotaurZombie`, `ZombieFactory.TroglodyteZombie`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/BaseCreatureDefinitions.cs (`Minotaur`, primary Gore); Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs (`GetNaturalAttackSequencePenalty`); MM p.266-267, p.312. *Fix:* always add the zombie slam as a primary attack: `DamageBonusSource.Strength` when the zombie has other natural attacks, `StrengthOneAndHalf` when the slam is its only one. Record the reading in docs/systems/RULES_COVERAGE.md 4.1, then check the spawned attack lines of the minotaur and troglodyte zombies in the editor. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-289** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Owlbear (`owlbear`) differs from its MM stat block (MM p.206).** Reach (IsTallCreature): the game has true, so the Large owlbear threatens and attacks at 10 ft, the MM space 10 ft with reach 5 ft (a Large long creature); it gains attacks of opportunity and attack options it should not have. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_O.cs (`Id = "owlbear"`); MM p.206. *Fix:* set IsTallCreature to false, then check the spawned reach in the editor and rerun the creature's static suites and the soak. Found by the MM stat-block audit of 2026-11-16 (reported as a formula finding and moved here; the skeleton version is CRE-251). Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-291** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Bestow Curse's ability penalty differs from the DMG (DMG p.290).** Penalty type: the game stores the -6 as ordinary ability damage (target.ApplyAbilityDamage), the DMG makes an effective ability score reduction a penalty and not ability damage; floor: the game floors the score at 0 (GetEffectiveAbilityScore) and runs CheckAbilityScoreZeroEffects, so a score of 6 or less reaches 0 and Con 0 dies or Str, Dex, Int, Wis or Cha 0 is helpless or comatose, the PHB p.203 gives a minimum of 1; removal: the game lets Restoration, Greater Restoration, Heal and Wish cure it (natural recovery would too, but it never runs in play, CORE-035) while the CurseTracker entry stays, and Remove Curse heals 6 points of that ability's damage from any source, the PHB allows only break enchantment, limited wish, miracle, remove curse or wish (SPL-229 covers only the action-loss option). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager_Spells_B.cs TryResolveBestowCurseSpellEffect (case AbilityPenalty, line 156, again line 229); Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs GetEffectiveAbilityScore (line 3561); Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager_Spells_R.cs (line 544); DMG p.290; PHB p.203. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-292** · **Med** · Player-visible — **4 rules of the DMG special ability glossary (ability score loss through charm and compulsion) have no rules path in play (DMG p.289-291, p.295-296).** Double ability damage recovery with complete bed rest (DMG p.289): ability damage returns at 1 point per day, 2 with complete bed rest; the game has CharacterStats.ApplyCompleteRest healing 1 per ability for any rest of 8 hours or more and EffectService.ProcessDailyEffects healing 1, with no code reading bed rest or doubling (neither path runs in play, CORE-035); blindsight and blindsense (DMG p.290-291, p.295-296): blindsight ignores invisibility, darkness, concealment, displacement and blur, so a creature with blindsight attacks and otherwise interacts with invisible creatures (except invisible incorporeal or ethereal ones, DMG p.296), and blindsense locates creatures in range without Spot or Listen; the game has only SpecialAbilities text (gelatinous cube, gray ooze, ochre jelly, black pudding, grimlock, destrachan) and CreatureTags ('Blindsight60', 'Blindsight100', 'Blindsight40', 'Sightless') that no code reads (GetMissChance, CanSee, HasTotalConcealment, PerceptionRules and LastKnownPositionTracker never test them; CharacterController.CanSeeInvisible is true only for an active See Invisibility effect; MirrorImageRules reads only 'Sightless'; CMB-182, CMB-183), so a creature with blindsight keeps the invisibility 50% miss chance against an invisible creature, cannot locate it, and the AI's invisible-target scoring (AIService.GetPerceptionTargetingAdjustment) treats it like a sighted creature (pinpointing an invisible creature by being struck or by groping: see CMB-303; by scent: see CRE-308); charm orders (DMG p.291): the charmer gives instructions, with an opposed Charisma check to resist orders, refusal of suicidal or harmful orders and a new save against an order the creature is violently opposed to; the game has no order action or request and CharmedConditionData holds only the caster, rounds and source, a charmed NPC follows CharmedBehaviorController's fixed behavior and only the break on an open hostile act (BreakCharmOnHostileAction) is built; compulsion by Dominate Person and Command Undead (DMG p.291, PHB p.224, p.211): the subject obeys the caster; the game stores only a CommandUndeadEffectData through ApplyCommandUndeadEffect, changing neither Team nor IsControllable, read only by CombatEndRules, the hostile-act break, dispel and post-combat rest, so a dominated or commanded foe keeps acting as an enemy on its AI turn and a dominated PC stays under the player's control (see CORE-014; SPL-140 is only the duration). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs ApplyCompleteRest (line 2143); Assets/Scripts/Spell/Effects/EffectService.cs ProcessDailyEffects; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs GetMissChance, CanSee, CanSeeInvisible, HasTotalConcealment, ApplyCommandUndeadEffect (line 3269); Assets/Scripts/Combat/Core/PerceptionRules.cs; Assets/Scripts/AI/LastKnownPositionTracker.cs; Assets/Scripts/Spell/Special/MirrorImageRules.cs IsSightless; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs, NPCDatabase_D.cs, NPCDatabase_G.cs, NPCDatabase_O.cs (Blindsight tags and text, unread); Assets/Scripts/Combat/Behaviors/CharmedBehaviorController.cs; Assets/Scripts/Combat/Conditions/CharmedConditionData.cs; Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager.SpellCasting.cs (Command Undead line 4564, Dominate Person lines 4644-4679); Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs GetPerceptionTargetingAdjustment; DMG p.289-291, p.295-296. *Fix:* build each rule in the shared code for PCs and NPCs alike, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Also filed as CRE-301, merged here 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-293** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Damage reduction differs from the DMG (DMG p.292).** special effects that accompany an attack DR fully absorbs (injury poison, injury disease, a monk's stunning): the game applies them on any hit, because `TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` tests only `attackResult.Hit` (never `FinalDamageDealt` or `DRPrevented`, though both are already set) and the Stunning Fist block tests only that the target is not dead, so a bite or sting whose damage DR cuts to 0 still poisons or infects (the poison delivery type `PoisonData.Type` is never read there and `DiseaseData` has no infection field) and a fully absorbed unarmed hit would still stun (latent while Stunning Fist cannot be used, CMB-022), the DMG says DR that completely negates the damage also negates these effects, but not touch attacks, energy damage dealt with the attack, energy drain, or inhaled, ingested or contact poison and disease; weapons given an alignment by the align weapon spell overcome DR of that alignment: the game has no rules path in play, because Align Weapon only sets `AlignWeaponActive` and `AlignWeaponAlignment` on the creature (`EffectService.cs:417-420`), `GameManager.CanBypassAlignmentDR` has no caller and `GetWeaponAttackBypassTags` adds alignment tags only from an enchanted weapon, so an attack under Align Weapon never overcomes DR/good, evil, lawful or chaotic for PCs and NPCs alike, the DMG says such a weapon overcomes DR of that alignment (align weapon, PHB p.197). *Evidence:* `CharacterController.TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` (`Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:6116`, poison and disease blocks); `CharacterController.PerformSingleAttackWithCritCore` (Stunning Fist block, :9028); `Assets/Scripts/Effects/Disease.cs` `DiseaseData`; `GameManager.CanBypassAlignmentDR` (`Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager_Spells_A.cs:82`); `CharacterController.GetWeaponAttackBypassTags` (:8135); DMG p.291-292. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-294** · **Low** — **Disease (save, incubation, daily saves, recovery) differs from the DMG (DMG p.292).** recovery rule of devil chills and mummy rot: the game cures every disease after two successful daily saves in a row (`ConsecutiveSuccessfulSaves >= 2`, `DiseaseData` has no per-disease recovery field), so devil chills is cured after 2 saves and mummy rot is cured by saves at all, the DMG devil chills needs three successful saves in a row and mummy rot is never cured by saves (they only prevent that day's damage; only magical healing cures it) (Table 8-2 notes 3 and 4; CRE-244 covers mummy rot's DC and damage only); first damage at the end of incubation: the game marks the disease active when `DaysUntilActive` reaches 0 with no damage, so the first dose comes a day later and only if that day's Fortitude save fails, the DMG a character who failed the initial save takes damage after incubation and only from then on does a daily save prevent it; extra effects of demon fever, slimy doom and blinding sickness: the game has no rules path in play, because every `DiseaseDatabase` entry leaves `SpecialEffects` empty so a failed daily save deals only plain ability damage, with no second save to turn 1 point into permanent drain (demon fever, slimy doom) and no Fortitude save against permanent blindness on 2 or more damage (blinding sickness), the DMG has these extra effects (Table 8-2 notes 1 and 2). Daily processing itself never runs in play (CORE-035). *Evidence:* `CharacterController.ProcessDiseaseEffectsDaily` (`Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:5346`; IsIncubating branch :5360-5375, cure :5384-5392); `Assets/Scripts/Effects/Disease.cs` `ActiveDisease.ConsecutiveSuccessfulSaves`; `DiseaseDatabase.Initialize` (DevilChills, MummyRot, BlindingSickness, SlimyDoom, DemonFever); DMG p.292. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-295** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Energy drain and negative levels (spell loss) differ from the DMG (DMG p.293).** spell loss from a negative level for sorcerers, bards, paladins and rangers: the game's `SpellcastingComponent.ApplyNegativeLevelSlotLoss` returns at once when `SpellSlots` is empty (that list serves only the prepared-slot casters wizard, cleric and druid), `RefreshSpontaneousSlots` sets a sorcerer's or bard's slots from the plain class level and nothing lowers them, and paladins and rangers lose nothing, the DMG a spellcaster with a negative level loses a spell as if she had cast her highest-level spell still available and gets one fewer slot at her highest spell level the next time she prepares or regains slots; which spell is lost: the game disables slots from level 9 down and never tests `slot.IsUsed`, so when the caster has already cast her top-level spell that spent slot is the one disabled and she loses nothing she still has until the next rest, the DMG a spell she still has. *Evidence:* `SpellcastingComponent.ApplyNegativeLevelSlotLoss` (`Assets/Scripts/Spell/Components/SpellcastingComponent.cs:2579`, :2590-2602); `SpellcastingComponent.RefreshSpontaneousSlots` (:824); DMG p.293. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-309** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Negative levels do not lower the effective level in level-based rolls and calculations (DMG p.293).** The game applies a negative level's -1 effective level only to caster level (`CharacterStats.GetCasterLevel` class caster level paths, `CasterLevelRules.HitDiceCasterLevel`), the casting Concentration proxy (`CharacterStats.GetConcentrationProxyLevel`) and spell-slot loss (CRE-295). `CharacterStats.EffectiveLevelAfterDrain` (Level - NegativeLevelCount, at least 1) has no reader, and every other level-based number reads the undrained level: sneak attack dice and damage (`CharacterController.PerformSingleAttackWithCritCore` and the coup de grace, `CharacterController.ResolveCoupDeGrace`, both call `CombatUtils.GetSneakAttackDice` and `CombatUtils.RollSneakAttackDamage` with `Stats.GetClassLevel("Rogue")`), the turning level that sets both the most Hit Dice affected and the 2d6 + level + Cha turning damage (`CharacterStats.TurnUndeadEffectiveLevel` through `GameManager.GetTurnUndeadEffectiveLevel`, read by the turn undead executor `GameManager.ActionCore_TurnUndead` and by `AIProfile`), smite evil damage (`SmiteRules.DamageBonus`, paladin level), the Stunning Fist DC (`FeatManager.GetStunningFistDC`, `CharacterStats.Level`; latent while Stunning Fist cannot be used, CMB-022), the lay on hands pool (`CharacterStats.LayOnHandsTotalPool`) and the monk's level-based numbers (`CharacterStats.MonkLevel`, read by `MonkACBonus`, `TryGetMonkUnarmedMediumDamage`, `MonkFastMovementBonusFeet` and `FlurryOfBlowsAttackPenalty`). The DMG says each negative level lowers the creature's level by one wherever that level is used in a die roll or calculation (DMG p.293), and these numbers are calculated from level: sneak attack dice from rogue level (PHB p.50), turning damage 2d6 + cleric level + Cha (PHB p.159), the Stunning Fist DC 10 + 1/2 character level + Wis (PHB p.101). Player-visible: a rogue, cleric, paladin or monk drained by a wight, vampire spawn or spectre, or by enervation, keeps full sneak attack dice, turning, smite damage, lay on hands pool and monk AC bonus for the rest of the fight (negative levels last only until the fight ends, CORE-036). The code is shared, so PCs and NPCs behave alike. *Evidence:* `Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs` (`EffectiveLevelAfterDrain` :1541, `TurnUndeadEffectiveLevel` :1491, `LayOnHandsTotalPool` :999, `MonkLevel` :1154, class caster level :2483, :2496, concentration proxy :2585); `Assets/Scripts/Spell/Casting/Core/CasterLevelRules.cs` (:125); `Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs` (sneak dice :8925, coup de grace :15409); `Assets/Scripts/Combat/Utilities/CombatUtils.cs` (`RollSneakAttackDamage` :170, `GetSneakAttackDice` :185); `Assets/Scripts/Combat/Special/TurnUndeadSystem.cs` (`GameManager.GetTurnUndeadEffectiveLevel` :413-428); `Assets/Scripts/Combat/Actions/Executors/GameManager.ClassAbilityActions.cs` (turning level :39, turning damage :100); `Assets/Scripts/AI/AIProfile.cs` (:602); `Assets/Scripts/Combat/Special/SmiteRules.cs` (`DamageBonus` :214); `Assets/Scripts/Character/Feats/FeatManager.cs` (`GetStunningFistDC` :531); DMG p.293. *Fix:* add one shared effective-level rule in `CharacterStats` (class level or character level less `NegativeLevelCount`) and make every level-based die roll and calculation read it: sneak attack dice, turning level and damage, smite damage, level-based save DCs, lay on hands and the monk's class-level formulas, for PCs and NPCs alike. Keep feature gates (whether a creature has a feature at all) on the real level, because the DMG names only die rolls and calculations, and record that reading in `docs/systems/RULES_COVERAGE.md` 4.1. Reuse or delete `EffectiveLevelAfterDrain`, add static tests, then rerun the rules scenarios. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-296** · **Med** · Player-visible — **3 rules of the DMG special ability glossary (cold, damage reduction, darkvision, death attacks, disease, energy drain, etherealness, evasion) have no rules path in play (DMG p.291-293).** death attacks and the death ward spell (DMG p.292, PHB p.217): the DMG says death ward protects against death attacks (by its spell text also death spells, magical death effects, energy drain and negative energy); the game only sets `DeathWardActive` and ticks it down, only the Death domain touch tests it (`GameManager.DeathWardBlocks`, batch 14 step 14, 2026-11-23: the touch is made and the day's use spent, and on a hit it has no effect on a warded creature), so Death Knell and `ApplyNegativeLevels` affect a warded creature normally (the slaying arrow is ITM-414 and the death touch button CHR-060); etherealness (DMG p.293): ethereal creatures are invisible, inaudible and insubstantial to Material creatures, only force, gaze and abjuration effects reach them, they cannot affect the Material Plane, see and hear 60 ft into it, move through objects in any direction and do not fall; the game has no ethereal state, and Ethereal Jaunt on the phase spider, ethereal filcher, ethereal marauder and others is SpecialAbilities text only with no action and no AI use (the armor Etherealness enchantment is ITM-360); evasion as an innate creature quality (DMG p.293): a creature with evasion takes no damage on a successful Reflex save for half, without the rogue's and monk's armor limit; the game's `HasEvasion` is true only for monk 2+, rogue 2+, ranger 9+ and a ring of evasion and `HasImprovedEvasion` only for monk 9+, `NPCDefinition` has no evasion field and nothing parses SpecialAbilities, so the giant eagle's Evasion never applies (CRE-094 does not cover it, the animal companion case is CHR-020). *Evidence:* `CharacterStats.DeathWardActive` (`Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs:4217`), `GameManager_Spells_D.TryResolveDeathWardSpellEffect` (:36), `TryResolveDeathKnellSpellEffect` (:328), `CharacterController.ApplyNegativeLevels` (:4639), `GameManager_DomainPowers.ActivateDeathTouch` (:265); `NPCDatabase_P.cs:87`, `NPCDatabase_E.cs:624`, `:658`, `NPCDatabase_C.cs:726`, `NPCDatabase_S.cs:629`; `CharacterStats.HasEvasion` and `HasImprovedEvasion` (:1273, :1280), `SpellSaveResolver.ApplyEvasion` (:372), `NPCDatabase_E.cs:102`; DMG p.292-293. *Fix:* build each rule in the shared code for PCs and NPCs alike. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-297** · **Low** — **Fear (frightened: flight ends once the source is out of sight or hearing) differs from the DMG (DMG p.294).** when a frightened creature must keep fleeing: the game makes a creature with a Frightened condition flee on every turn for the whole duration while its source is alive, whether or not it can still see or hear that source (`EvaluateTurnCompulsion` keeps the fear whenever the source resolves and is alive, `FlightRules.Sources` has no sight or hearing test, `FrightenedBehaviorController.ExecuteDecision` always runs Flee, and the PC `TryRunCompulsionTurn` and the AI `compulsion.Fear` branch do the same; nothing reads CanSee, concealment or Listen; it shows only when the source is truly out of sight, such as behind total concealment or invisible, since the game has no line-of-sight blocking, GRID-009; CMB-178 covers only a dead or missing source), the DMG a frightened creature flees from the source of its fear and may choose its path, and once out of sight (or hearing) of the source it may act as it wants, fleeing again only if the fear still runs and the source shows itself again. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/_Core/GameManager.TurnStart.cs `EvaluateTurnCompulsion` and `TryRunCompulsionTurn`; Assets/Scripts/Combat/Behaviors/FlightRules.cs `Sources`; Assets/Scripts/Combat/Behaviors/FrightenedBehaviorController.cs `ExecuteDecision` and `Flee`; Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs (compulsion.Fear branch); DMG p.294. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-298** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Fear (panicked: random path, flee from all other dangers) differs from the DMG (DMG p.294).** dangers a panicked creature flees from: the game runs the same Flee routine as for a frightened creature, and flees only from its one fear source (`FindBestFleeCell` scores each cell only by its distance from that source's square; other threats count only as a tie-break that prefers the path provoking fewer attacks of opportunity among cells at the same distance; `FlightRules.Sources` returns only the fear source, and the turner when turned), so a panicked creature can flee toward or past other enemies, or stay beside them, the DMG a panicked creature runs from the source of its fear as fast as it can, also flees from all other dangers that confront it instead of facing them, and cowers if it cannot flee. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Combat/Behaviors/FrightenedBehaviorController.cs `Flee` and `FindBestFleeCell`; Assets/Scripts/Combat/Behaviors/FlightRules.cs `Sources`; DMG p.294. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-299** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Incorporeality (cannot be grappled) differs from the DMG (DMG p.295).** grapple against an incorporeal creature: the game lets a corporeal PC or NPC make a touch attack and an opposed grapple check against a wraith, shadow, spectre, allip or ghost, and hold it (`CharacterController.CanTrip` and the bull rush gate refuse an incorporeal target and attacker, but `ResolveGrapple`, the grapple special attack for PCs and NPCs, checks only swarm, a summoned contact barrier, `CanUseStandardGrapple` and existing grapple links, and `ResolveImprovedGrabFreeAttempt`, reached through `GameManager.ResolveImprovedGrabChoice`, has no test either; `IsIncorporeal` is read only by the trip and bull rush gates and the movement terrain exemptions; `UndeadIncorporealAIProfile` only stops incorporeal NPCs from choosing a grapple; CMB-250 does not cover it), the DMG corporeal creatures cannot trip or grapple incorporeal creatures. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `ResolveGrapple`, `ResolveImprovedGrabFreeAttempt`, compare `CanTrip` and the bull rush gate; DMG p.295. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-300** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Incorporeality and invisibility (no extra damage from favored enemy) differ from the DMG (DMG p.295).** ranger favored enemy damage against an incorporeal target: the game adds `FavoredEnemyBonus` (2 at ranger 1-4, the best matching entry) to every weapon hit, including magic-weapon hits on a wraith, shadow, spectre or allip (`PerformSingleAttackWithCritCore` sets `damageBonus.FavoredEnemyBonus` from `RangerRules.FavoredEnemyDamageBonus`, and the grapple damage and coup de grace paths do the same; it tests only creature type and subtypes, never `IsIncorporeal`; the delegated reading CHR-020 covers type identification, not this immunity), the DMG incorporeal creatures are immune to extra damage from being a favored enemy (0); ranger favored enemy damage against an invisible target: the game adds the same bonus when the target is invisible to the attacker (it has an invisibility effect and the attacker has no `CanSeeInvisible`; nothing in `FavoredEnemyDamageBonus` or its callers reads invisibility), the DMG invisibility makes a creature immune to the extra damage from being a ranger's favored enemy (0), though not to critical hits. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `PerformSingleAttackWithCritCore`, grapple damage (`RollAndApplyGrappleCheckDamage`), coup de grace (`ResolveCoupDeGrace`); Assets/Scripts/Character/Classes/Ranger/RangerRules.cs `FavoredEnemyBonus` and `FavoredEnemyDamageBonus`; DMG p.295. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-302** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Poison (using poison) differs from the DMG (DMG p.296).** injury poison on a hit that deals no damage (see CRE-293); applying and using a dose of poison: the game has no rules path (poison reaches a creature only through natural-attack `PoisonOnHitId` and a few items and spells, while the swarm poison rider in `AIService` only logs the exposure; `PoisonDatabase` holds DMG poisons such as black adder venom, bloodroot and greenblood oil, but there is no poison item in `ItemDatabase` or the store, no action to smear a dose on a weapon, no dose kept until the weapon hits or the object is touched, no 5% chance of poisoning oneself when applying or readying it, and no thrown inhaled vial with a 10 ft range increment, a 10 ft cube and a save for each creature; `PoisonData.Type` is read only to choose the secondary damage delay and to name the type in the exposure log), the DMG one dose on a weapon or object affects a single target and stays until the weapon hits or the object is touched, with a 5% chance of self-exposure when applying or readying it and separate delivery rules for contact, ingested, inhaled and injury poisons. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `TryApplyNaturalAttackOnHitEffects` and `ApplyPoison`; Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs (swarm poison rider); Assets/Scripts/Effects/Poison.cs; Assets/Scripts/Effects/PoisonDatabase.cs; Assets/Scripts/Equipment/Items/ItemDatabase.cs (no poison items); DMG p.296. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-303** · **Med** · Player-visible — **1 rule of the DMG special ability glossary (evasion through paralysis) has no rules path in play (DMG p.293-296).** gaseous form (DMG p.294): a gaseous creature has DR 10/magic, cannot run but can fly, makes no physical attacks and casts no spells with verbal, somatic, material or focus components, loses supernatural abilities and material and natural armor (size, Dex, deflection and force armor bonuses stay), needs no air and is immune to attacks that work through breathing (such as stench and poison gas), cannot enter water, is moved by wind, and is told from mist by Spot DC 15 and gets +20 on Hide in mist or fog; the game has nothing (gaseous form exists only as SpecialAbilities text on the vampire spawn, vampire, efreeti and entries in NPCDatabase_G, NPCDatabase_D and NPCDatabase_O, with no `SpellNames` entry, `SpellData` or handler, and the treasure potion "Gaseous form (potion)" has no effect behind it). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_V.cs (vampire_spawn and vampire SpecialAbilities) and the other SpecialAbilities lines; no `SpellNames.GASEOUS_FORM`; Assets/Scripts/TreasureGenerator/TreasureData.cs (potion row); DMG p.294. *Fix:* build each rule in the shared code for PCs and NPCs alike. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-304** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Poison (immunity to a creature's own poison; delivery types) differs from the DMG (DMG p.296-297).** immunity to a creature's own poison: the game has `CharacterStats.IsImmuneToPoison` check only `Immunities.immuneToPoison`, wondrous poison immunity, venom immunity and the types undead, construct, ooze, elemental and plant, and `CharacterController.ApplyPoison` checks nothing else (nothing compares the incoming poison id with the target's own `NaturalAttacks` `PoisonOnHitId`, and the wyvern and medusa set no poison immunity), so a same-kind creature, such as a summoned fiendish monstrous centipede or a confused or charmed member of the poisoner's group, rolls the saves and takes the ability damage from its own kind's poison, the DMG a creature with a natural poison attack is immune to its own poison; delivery type of three poisons: the game types `carrion_crawler_brain_juice` as Injury, `dragon_bile` as Ingested and `ungol_dust` as Ingested in `PoisonDatabase`, the DMG Table 8-3 contact, contact and inhaled. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs `IsImmuneToPoison`; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `ApplyPoison`; Assets/Scripts/Effects/PoisonDatabase.cs (the three poison entries); DMG p.296-297. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-305** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Regeneration differs from the DMG (DMG p.298).** damage is treated as nonlethal: the game deals ordinary lethal damage to a regenerating creature (troll 5, ogre mage 5, chain devil 2; `CharacterStats.ApplyIncomingDamage` and `TakeDamage` convert nothing) and `CharacterController.ApplyRegenerationAtTurnStart` heals nonlethal damage then lethal HP at turn start like fast healing (returning early at CurrentHP <= -10; only the Ring of Regeneration's `RegenerationEffect.CheckDeathPrevention` prevents death, which creatures never get), so a troll dropped below 0 HP by a sword or a spell is dying and dies at -10, the DMG damage to a regenerating creature is nonlethal and cured at the fixed rate each round, so ordinary damage only knocks it unconscious (CRE-017 covers only the mephits); fire and acid (the forms that bypass regeneration): the game suppresses regeneration only for a packet whose `AttackTags` share a bit with `_regenerationSuppressedBy` (a `DamageBypassTag`, material or alignment) and then only skips the next turn's heal, never reading the damage type, and `NPCDatabase_T.cs` gives the troll `DamageBypassTag.None` (comment "Fire and Acid suppress", no such code) while `NPCDatabase_O.cs` sets nothing for the ogre mage, so fire or acid is regenerated like any damage, the DMG fire and acid (or the forms the entry names) deal normal damage that is not converted to nonlethal and can kill (CRE-174 and CRE-139 cover the chain devil and lemure only); instant-death attacks: the game's coup de grace (`CharacterController`, the "Coup de Grace" Fortitude block) sets CurrentHP to -10 on a failed save with no regeneration check, so a sword coup de grace kills a helpless troll (massive damage does not exist, CMB-071), the DMG an instant-death attack threatens a regenerating creature only when delivered by a weapon that deals it lethal damage. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `ApplyRegenerationAtTurnStart`, `NotifyIncomingDamage`, `ConfigureRegeneration`, the coup de grace block; Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs `ApplyIncomingDamage`, `TakeDamage`; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_T.cs (troll), NPCDatabase_O.cs (ogre_mage); DMG p.298. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-306** · **Low** — **Spell resistance (a creature's own spells) differs from the DMG (DMG p.298).** a creature's spell resistance interferes with its own spells: the game rolls `SpellSaveResolver.RollSpellResistance` in `SpellCaster.Cast` whenever the spell is SR-yes and the target's SR is above 0, with no exemption when the target is the caster (`GameManager.CastStageResolve` runs every single-target PC and NPC cast through it), so a caster with SR that casts an SR-yes spell on itself rolls d20 + caster level + Spell Penetration against its own SR and can lose the spell (SPL-176 covers only an ally's SR against a harmless spell), the DMG a creature's spell resistance never interferes with its own spells, items or abilities. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Spell/Casting/SpellCaster.cs `Cast` (spell resistance block, about lines 359-378); Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager.CastCore.cs `CastStageResolve`; DMG p.298. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-307** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Spell resistance (ongoing effects) differs from the DMG (DMG p.299).** spell resistance against an ongoing area spell when the creature is first affected: the game has no `PersistentAreaEffect` subclass that rolls spell resistance or keeps a per-casting record of who overcame it (the only area effect with an SR roll is `GustOfWindEffect`; Spike Stones sets `SpellResistanceApplies` but `TryResolveSpikeStoneSpell` only creates the zone and `SpikeStoneAreaEffect` deals piercing damage through `DealSpellDamage` with no SR check; Wall of Ice likewise in `WallOfIceAreaEffect`; Wall of Fire and Flaming Sphere are filed under SPL-315), the DMG spell resistance is checked the first time a creature is affected by a spell already in place and only once per casting, a creature that resisted ignores that casting afterwards and one that did not is always affected by it. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Spell/AreaEffects/PersistentAreaEffect.cs (no SR hook or per-casting record); SpikeStoneAreaEffect.cs; WallOfIceAreaEffect.cs; Assets/Scripts/Spell/Resolution/GameManager_DomainAreaSpells.cs `TryResolveSpikeStoneSpell`; DMG p.299. *Fix:* make the rule match the DMG in the shared, actor-agnostic code that the PC UI and the AI both call, then rerun the static suites and rules scenarios that cover it. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-308** · **Med** · Player-visible — **2 rules of the DMG special ability glossary (scent and tremorsense) have no rules path in play (DMG p.298-299).** Scent (DMG p.298): detects opponents by smell within about 30 ft (60 upwind, 15 downwind; strong scents at twice and overpowering at three times), notices a presence but not its location, noting the direction is a move action and moving within 5 ft pinpoints it, follows a trail with the Track feat (DC 10 fresh, +2 per hour), and a powerful false odor masks other scents; the game has only a flag (`NPCSetup` copies `HasScent` to `CharacterStats.HasScent`, and the sole rules reader `AIService.GetPerceptionTargetingAdjustment` adds +4 to an invisible target's score within 6 squares and -6 beyond, -18 without scent), with no detection range, wind, direction, pinpointing, tracking or masking, no scent term in `CanSee`, `CanVisuallyPerceive` or the concealment code, and the druid wild-shape forms list Scent only as `SpecialAbilities` text (RULES_COVERAGE 3.2 marks it Minimal); Tremorsense (DMG p.299): automatically senses the location of anything within range that touches the ground, measured along the ground path, the sensing creature must itself touch the ground and the sensed ones must be taking physical actions; the game has nothing (it appears only as creature tags such as Tremorsense30 and `SpecialAbilities` text in NPCDatabase_A, B, C, P, S, T, U and X, plus a comment in `MirrorImageRules`; `CanSee`, `CanVisuallyPerceive`, `GetMissChance` and the AI never let a creature locate a ground-contact creature, so it gets nothing against an invisible or hidden foe). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Stats/CharacterStats.cs `HasScent`; Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs `GetPerceptionTargetingAdjustment`; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs `CanSee`, `CanVisuallyPerceive`, `GetMissChance`; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_*.cs (tags and text only); DMG p.298-299. *Fix:* build each rule in the shared code for PCs and NPCs alike. Found by the PHB and DMG rules audit of 2026-11-17. Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-310** · **Low** · Player-visible — **The hobgoblin sergeant has Combat Expertise with Int 10 and two feats for Fighter 3.** `hobgoblin_sergeant` (a custom creature in `NPCDatabaseCustom.cs`, `RegisterHobgoblinSergeant`) is a Fighter 3 with Int 10 and the feats Weapon Focus and Combat Expertise. Combat Expertise needs Int 13 (PHB p.92), and a 3rd-level fighter has four feats: one at 1st and 3rd level and fighter bonus feats at 1st and 2nd (PHB p.37-38). The AI and the PC panel both offer it Combat Expertise (the defensive routine uses it). Found while deriving batch 14's `hobgoblin_captain` from it (2026-11-23); the captain has Int 13 and four feats. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabaseCustom.cs (`RegisterHobgoblinSergeant`). *Fix:* raise Int to 13 (a DMG p.110 elite array arrangement) or replace the feat, and give it two more feats; the change moves the hashes of the scenarios that use it (grep the catalog for `hobgoblin_sergeant`). Not verified in Play mode.
- **CRE-311** · **Low** · Player-visible — **A summoned creature that vanishes while grappling stays its opponent's grapple partner for the rest of the current turn.** When a summon's duration ends (the expiry tick can fire inside the opposing creature's turn start), `GameManager.DespawnSummonWithEffect` (`Spell/Resolution/GameManager.SpellCasting.cs`) takes it off the grid, out of `NPCs`, `_npcAIBehaviors` and the initiative, logs that it disappears and calls `Destroy`, all at once since grouped AI turns step 4 (CRE-312, 2026-11-24; before it, the removal waited on the 0.45 s despawn effect, now played by the summon's `TokenView`). Unity defers `Destroy` to the end of the frame, and the grapple is released only by `CharacterController.OnDestroy` (`ReleaseGrappleState("character removed")`), so the rest of the turn already running in that frame still sees the grapple: in the batch 14 final soak (2026-11-23, run 20261123-220926, seed 48, trace `Logs/Scenarios/20261123-220926/soak_dmg-random-encounters__s48.jsonl`, round 12) the werewolf's turn began, the party wizard's fiendish monstrous scorpion disappeared as its Summon Monster II ended, and the werewolf then spent its turn on a grapple attack against the scorpion (a miss) before both Grappled conditions were removed (the log, the attack and the removals all in frame 156). RAW: when a summoning spell ends, the summoned creature is instantly sent back (PHB p.173, Conjuration (Summoning)), so nothing held the werewolf when its turn began; the game's own release on removal (`ReleaseGrappleState("character removed")`) agrees and simply comes late. *Evidence:* the trace above (seq 390-398); `DespawnSummonWithEffect`; `CharacterController.OnDestroy`. *Fix:* in `DespawnSummonWithEffect`, call `cc.ReleaseGrappleState(...)` (public on `CharacterController`) and clear the other links `OnDestroy` clears (the feint windows and indicators) synchronously, before `Destroy`; CRE-312's synchronous removal alone does not fix it, since the release still waits for the end-of-frame `OnDestroy`. Not verified by hand in Play mode.

## Unreachable or stubbed features

- **CRE-011** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Lycanthropes are hybrid-only and the curse does nothing.** Form shifting and curse progression are not implemented, and natural lycanthrope bites carry `DiseaseType.Lycanthropy`, which has no `DiseaseDatabase` entry, so `CharacterController.ExposeToDisease` silently skips the DC 15 save that the SpecialAbilities text advertises. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Templates/LycanthropeTemplate.cs:290-296; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:4781-4785. *Fix:* add a Lycanthropy DiseaseData entry (DC 15 Fort) or a curse handler, and log unknown on-hit disease types.
- **CRE-012** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Several Monster Manual abilities are placeholders or text only.** The Beholder's "Antimagic Cone" is a Frightened aura (SaveDC 0, 0 rounds) that suppresses no magic, the Vargouille's Shriek frightens instead of paralyzing, and the juvenile Arrowhawk has no electricity ray; orc and kobold light sensitivity and the summoned Unicorn's SLAs and magic circle are text only. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_B.cs:842-896, NPCDatabase_V.cs:342-377, NPCDatabase_U.cs:63-108. *Fix:* implement an antimagic zone or remove the aura, give the Vargouille a Paralyzed aura, and implement or relabel the Unicorn's abilities.
- **CRE-013** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Gibbering Mouther Ground Manipulation does nothing.** `AIService.ActivateTerrainManipulation` only logs and never marks difficult terrain, and it runs after every AI advance move, so the "activates ..." message repeats each turn; the duration tick now runs at every turn start (`TickTurnCooldowns`, AI-032), but it only counts down a counter set at spawn that nothing reads, since `IsTerrainManipulationActive` has no caller. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs:873-874, 4305-4321; Assets/Scripts/Character/Controller/CharacterController.cs:4830-4835, 4936, 5190-5200. *Fix:* mark cells within `RadiusFeet` with `GameManager.SetAreaDifficultTerrain` (clearing them on expiry or move), activate once (starting the duration then) and read the active check.
- **CRE-014** · **Med** · Player-visible — **Swarm poison is never applied.** The swarm-damage poison rider in `AIService` only logs "X is exposed to Y's poison!" and never calls the existing `CharacterController.ApplyPoison(poisonId)`; in current data only the spider swarm (`medium_spider_poison`) is affected. *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Services/AIService.cs:1937-1941; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_S.cs:155-157. *Fix:* call `victim.ApplyPoison(traits.PoisonId)`, respecting poison immunity.
- **CRE-015** · **Low** · Player-visible — **Dragon spell-like abilities are never used.** `DragonData.PopulateSpellcastingData` fills per-age `SpellLikeAbilityIds`, but `NPCDatabase_Dragons` reads only `SorcererSpellIds`, so spawned dragons have no SLAs; the `DragonTypeTemplate` lists are never read either. Several SLAs are stand-ins anyway (Suggestion as charm_person, Corrupt Water as create_water). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs:109, 150, 1306-1326, 1526ff; Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase_Dragons.cs:103-108. *Fix:* map SpellLikeAbilityIds into uses-per-day SLAs and delete the template-level lists.
- **CRE-016** · **Low** — **Dragon spellcasting and age range are approximate.** `GetSpellsForCasterLevel` takes the first 6/9/13/16 entries of a flat list that starts with six cantrips, ignoring the sorcerer spells-known table (PHB Table 3-16: a 1st-level sorcerer knows four 0-level and two 1st-level spells), and `DragonAgeCategory` ends at Adult. Since batch 12 step 24 (2026-10-29) the caster level is stored on the definition (`NPCDefinition.InnateSpellcastingClass` and `InnateCasterLevel`, from the MM p.68-69 age table; the spawn no longer scans every dragon) and the injected sorcerer entry adds no character level (`CharacterStats.Level` stays the Hit Dice; `IsInnateSpellcastingEntry`), so the Level readers no longer over-scale for dragons. The spells known are deferred to batch 19 (owner decision Q3, 2026-10-23). *Evidence:* Assets/Scripts/Character/Creatures/DragonData.cs (`GetSpellsForCasterLevel`, `DragonAgeCategory`). *Fix:* store the spells per level and pick them by the sorcerer table; add the ages past Adult when the scope reaches them.
